# Implementation plan

Epics, tickets, acceptance criteria, dependencies, order and status. This file is the record of the work that travels with the repo to the course repository (GitHub issues and PRs do not), so keep each ticket's status current: every PR updates the status of its ticket.

**Status of this plan:** approved by the owner on 2026-10-19; GitHub issues #1-#20 created the same day (one per ticket). Epic 7 (T7.1-T7.3, issues #33-#35) added by the owner on 2026-10-20; T7.4 (issue #63) split from T7.1 by the owner on 2026-10-21. Epic 8 (the Sprint 2 review's follow-ups) and Epic 9 (the Sprint 3 agent) added by the owner on 2026-10-25, from `docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`; GitHub issues #90-#106 created the same day (one per ticket), and #107 for the PR that added them. Epic 9 rewritten by T9.1 on 2026-10-25 for the coach-first scope the owner chose that day (`docs/PRD.md`, "Sprint 3"): T9.4-T9.7 and T9.11 reworked, T9.12-T9.18 added (issues #112-#118). T9.19 and T9.20 added on 2026-10-26 for the training principles the owner chose that day (PRD feature 26), and T9.14, T9.16 and T9.11 changed with them; the owner approved them the same day, and their issues, NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#30 and #31, and #32 for the PR that added them, were created in https://github.com/NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant, where the work continues; these three are always written with the repository name, because a bare `#30`-`#32` in this plan is an issue of the personal repository named below. T9.22 and T9.23 added on 2026-10-30 from the owner's UI decisions of that day, and T9.15 changed with them; the owner approved their criteria the same day, and their issues, NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#52 and #53, were created then. T9.24 added on 2026-11-02 from the owner's decisions of that day (the coach-first rework: gymers without the AI chat; issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#60): T9.7, T9.11, T9.13, T9.15, T9.17 and T9.18 reworked, and T9.25 planned (its issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#61). T9.26 added on 2026-11-04 at the owner's request (a Capstone plan recorded in `docs/PRD.md`, docs only; issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#67), the owner approving its criteria the same day. T9.27 and T9.28 added on 2026-11-06 at the owner's request (README images and a presentation video, beyond the brief), the owner approving their criteria the same day; their issues are NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#71 and NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#72, and NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#73 for the PR that adds them; in the same PR T9.11a (NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#75) is split from T9.11, as the owner chose to do T9.11 in two PRs with T9.27 between them. T9.29 added on 2026-11-06 at the owner's request (in-app notifications for coaches and gymers, beyond the brief; moved from the Capstone, where only the realtime chat's notifications stay), the owner approving its criteria the same day; its issue is NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#76, and NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#77 for the PR that adds it. T1.12 added on 2026-11-06 by T9.9 (a follow-up found in passing; issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#84), its criteria awaiting the owner's approval. T9.30 added on 2026-11-07 by T9.25 (code review round 4 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#80; the owner chose a new ticket for the notifications of a new question or answer), its criteria approved by the owner on 2026-11-08 (with the middle way for the audit of a coach's question notifications); its issue is NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#87. T9.11b split from T9.11 on 2026-11-09 by the owner (the copy to the Sprint 3 course repository; issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#99). It follows `docs/PRD.md` (acceptance criteria AC-1 to AC-43; AC-43, for T9.29, added with it) and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (decisions D1-D22; D23 is added by T9.29).

**Numbers with `#`:** `#<n>` (an issue or pull request, here and in commit messages) refers to the personal repository where the work started, https://github.com/NMH1988/nutrition-ai-assistant (issue `#20` is https://github.com/NMH1988/nutrition-ai-assistant/issues/20); since 2026-10-26 the work continues in https://github.com/NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant, whose issues and PRs are mostly written with the repository name (`NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#<n>`); a bare PR or issue number in a ticket worked there, or in a commit message from 2026-10-26 on (every "Merge pull request #n" from T8.3's #29 on (T8.2's #121, merged the same morning, is the old repository's)), may be one of its numbers (e.g. "PR #37" in T1.11's ticket is T1.10's PR); issue and PR numbers in the course repository, if it has any, are unrelated. A source number (e.g. "sources #1 and #9", "#18a") is a row in `docs/SOURCES.md`.

## Status legend

`todo` · `in progress` · `in review` · `done` · `dropped` (with the reason) · `moved to the Capstone` (with the decision; the section stays as the record)

## Ticket format

```
### T<epic>.<n> <title> (S/M/L) · status: todo · issue #<n>
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): <core n | Easy, Medium or Hard n | evaluation item | Sprint 2 review point n> or none; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): <core n | Easy, Medium or Hard n | evaluation item> or none | beyond the brief (owner's request)
**Depends on:** T<x.y>, O<n>, ...
**Acceptance criteria**
- [ ] <testable criterion> - verified by <test or manual check>
**Notes:** files, risks
```

Sizes: S = up to half a day, M = about a day, L = more than a day.

## Overview and order

Deadline: **2026-11-02** (Sprint 2, already submitted by the owner, 2026-10-28). Target dates leave 1-2 days of buffer.

**Sprint 3** (deadline **2026-11-23**; owner's order, 2026-10-25): T9.1 first, then Epic 8's T1.8, T8.2, T8.3 and T8.4, then the Epic 9 P0 tickets in the table's order, then the "should" tickets (T9.17 first, raised from P1 by T9.24, then T9.8, T9.10), then P1 (T9.25; T9.18 moved to the Capstone by T9.24), then the extras (T9.9, T8.5, T8.6, and T9.29 after T9.16 and before T9.27, added on 2026-11-06, then T9.30 after T9.25 and T9.29 and before T9.27, added on 2026-11-07), and T9.11 last, done in two PRs with T9.27 between them (owner's choice, 2026-11-06): T9.11a (the app's title, intro and chat texts, split from T9.11), then T9.27, then T9.11 (the README work and the copy to the course repository; if T9.27 is cut, T9.11 drops that dependency and follows T9.11a); T9.28 is the one ticket that may come after T9.11 (T9.27 and T9.28 added on 2026-11-06). The targets assume about 12 hours of work a day and leave about a week of buffer. Estimate: P0 about 96-132 hours (Epic 8's four tickets included), should 15-21 (T9.17's 5-7 included since T9.24), P1 5-8 (T9.25; was 11-15 for T9.17 and T9.18), extras 10-15, plus T9.27 and T9.28 about 12-20, T9.29 about 6-9 and T9.30 about 3-5 (beyond the brief, from the buffer). **If time runs short, cut in this order:** T9.30 (added on 2026-11-07; it needs T9.29), T9.29, T9.28, T9.27 (all three added on 2026-11-06), T9.9, T8.6, T8.5, T9.25, T9.10, T9.8, T9.17 (T9.24: T9.17 now goes last, as the first ticket after P0; was T9.9, T8.6, T8.5, T9.18, T9.17, T9.10, T9.8); the P0 tickets stay, as they carry Sprint 3's core requirements and its optional tasks for maximum points (Medium 2, Medium 4, Hard 1). **Training principles** (owner's decision, 2026-10-26): T9.19 and T9.20 are P0, about 14-22 hours more, taken from the week of buffer; T9.19 runs once T8.4's baseline exists (owner's decision, 2026-10-28: now, before T9.3 and T9.4, as its only hard dependencies are T8.4 and the owner tasks O7, O16 and O17; it was planned after T9.4), and T9.20 after T9.3 and before T9.16; T9.19 merges only right before T9.20, as the answer prompt turns training chunks into advice, programs included, until T9.20's rules are in it (owner's choice, 2026-10-28). They are beyond the brief, so if time runs short they are cut after T9.8 and before the other P0 tickets. **The owner's UI decisions of 2026-10-30** (T9.22, T9.23 and T9.15's phone cards; owner's choice, 2026-10-30): about 1-1.5 days more, taken from the buffer; T9.22 and T9.23 run before T9.5. If time runs short, T9.23 and T9.15's card criterion are cut first, before T9.9 (the app keeps today's theme and the header's language control; T9.15 then drops its dependency on T9.23), and T9.22 stays, as it is small. **T9.20 first:** the AI-disclosure sentence of its About and disclaimer criterion moves to T9.11 (the disclosure covers the nutrition chat too, so it is not dropped with T9.20); O8 then leaves the training rules out; while T9.19 stays unmerged, no training chunk is in an index built from `main`, which keeps training advice out of the chat, O15 and T9.16's validation allow no training recommendation (a recommendation of kind "training" fails), and the rule of 2026-10-25 (training reported as facts only) applies again in the PRD. **Then T9.19's sources and indexing:** T9.19a's injury guard stays, as it is small and protects the chat too; T9.19 itself is not merged (without T9.20 the chat would write training programs from its chunks), or merges only with its training chunks kept out of retrieval; T9.16 drops its dependency on T9.19.

**The coach-first rework** (T9.24; owner's decisions, 2026-11-02, while trying T9.5's sign-in on PR NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#59): the AI serves the coach and a gymer has no AI chat. The gymer's chat tools (`get_my_week`, `remember_preference` for gymers) and logging by chat (T9.18) move to the Capstone; T9.7 becomes long-term memory for the coach; T9.17 is raised from P1 to "should", right after the P0 tickets; T9.25 (questions to the coach under an approved review) is the new P1 ticket, after T9.16. The P0 tickets T9.7, T9.13 and T9.15 are reworked, not enlarged, so the estimate for P0 does not change (T9.24 itself, docs only, comes from the buffer); Sprint 3 still reaches its optional tasks for maximum points ("For maximum points" in Epic 9).

**README images and the presentation video** (T9.27, T9.28; owner's request, 2026-11-06; beyond the brief): images and a video that show recruiters what the app does, as the app runs locally only and is not deployed (hosting cost, owner's decision, 2026-11-06). Both are extras, made once the tickets that change the UI are done (T9.11a's title and chat texts included), so the images match the app the reviewer sees; T9.27 lands after T9.11a and before T9.11, so the copy to the course repository carries its images. If time runs short they are cut right after T9.29 (added later the same day), T9.28 before T9.27, before T9.9; T9.28 may also be done after 2026-11-23, as it is for recruiters, not for the review.

**In-app notifications** (T9.29; owner's request, 2026-11-06; beyond the brief): a coach and a gymer see in the app what changed for them (a new client, a scope turned on or off, an approved weekly review, a relationship that ended), shown on the page's next run, with no email, push or real time. The PRD had them in the Capstone with full messaging (T9.24); the owner moved these to Sprint 3 on 2026-11-06, and only the notifications of the Capstone's realtime chat stay there. T9.29 lands after T9.16 and before T9.27, so the README images show it; after T9.30 it is the first ticket cut if time runs short (T9.30 needs it, owner's choice of 2026-11-07 to plan the question and answer kinds as T9.30), and T9.27 then captures the pages without it.

**Core first, then extras** (owner's decision, 2026-10-19): the brief's core requirements and the optional tasks needed for maximum points (Sprint 2: Medium 3, Medium 6, Hard 1) are finished first. The extras (Medium 5 token cost, Medium 7 PDF, the stretch tool, and Epic 7: English/German and light/dark mode) come after them and are still wanted, but never delay a core ticket. T6.2 comes last.

**If time runs short, cut in this order:** T4.5, then T7.2 (keep the English answers), then T7.1, then T7.4 (German input still reaches an English UI), then T7.3 (both themes stay from T3.1; only its test, toolbar check and hint go), then T5.2 (or keep it with a plain text layout), then T5.1, then chart polish. T7.3 is beyond the brief, so it goes before T5.2 and T5.1, which the brief asks for (Medium 7, Medium 5). The core requirements and the optional tasks for maximum points (Sprint 2: Medium 3, 6; Hard 1) stay.

## Epic 1: Foundation

### T1.1 Project skeleton, config and CI (S) · status: done · issue #1
**Serves:** core 4 (technical base); CI as in Sprint 1.
**Depends on:** -
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `pyproject.toml` pins the runtime packages from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 12 and the dev tools (pytest, pytest-cov, ruff, pip-audit); `.python-version` is 3.14; `pip install -e ".[dev]"` works on a clean venv - verified by CI install step.
- [x] `streamlit run app.py` shows the app title and the "not medical advice" disclaimer - verified by an AppTest smoke test.
- [x] `src/config.py` holds the model allowlist, default models (answer `openai/gpt-5-mini`, query translation `openai/gpt-5-nano`), limits and `get_api_key()`, which reads `st.secrets` or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` and returns a clear "missing key" error without printing the key - verified by unit tests with a fake key and with no key.
- [x] `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .`, `pytest` with coverage and `pip-audit` on push to `main` and on PRs, with no API key - verified by a green CI run.
- [x] `CLAUDE.md` "Commands" lists the real commands.
**Notes:** check every new package's licence (`.claude/rules/copyright.md` rule 9). Add `dependabot.yml` as in Sprint 1.

### T1.2 OpenRouter clients and error mapping (S) · status: done · issue #2
**Serves:** core 4 (LangChain with OpenRouter; error handling).
**Depends on:** T1.1
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `src/llm.py` builds the chat model (`ChatOpenAI` with OpenRouter's base URL, model from the allowlist, capped `max_tokens`) and the embeddings client (`OpenAIEmbeddings`, `qwen/qwen3-embedding-8b`, `check_embedding_ctx_length=False`) - verified by unit tests on the built objects' settings.
- [x] 401, 402, 429, timeout and 5xx map to distinct user-readable errors; retries are bounded; the key never appears in an error message - verified by unit tests with a mocked transport.
- [x] One real chat call and one real embedding call succeed - owner live check (O5) with `python scripts/live_check_llm.py` on 2026-10-19, recorded in `docs/TESTING.md`.

### T1.3 Input guard and rate limit (S) · status: done · issue #3
**Serves:** core 4 (input validation), Medium 3, AC-11.
**Depends on:** T1.1
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] Empty or whitespace-only input and input over the configured length are rejected with a clear message - unit tests (`tests/test_guard.py`: `test_empty_or_whitespace_is_rejected`, `test_input_over_the_limit_is_rejected`, `test_input_at_the_limit_passes`, `test_length_is_counted_after_cleaning`).
- [x] Common prompt-injection patterns are rejected; a set of benign nutrition questions passes (no false positives) - parametrised unit tests with attack and benign strings (`test_injection_attempts_are_rejected`, 104 attacks; `test_benign_questions_pass`, 81 questions, including advice from coaches, labels and official guidelines and the generic "you").
- [x] The per-session rate limit rejects requests above the limit - unit test with a fake clock (`test_rate_limit_rejects_above_the_window_limit`, `test_rate_limit_window_rolls`, `test_rate_limit_rejects_above_the_session_limit`).
- [x] A rejected input makes no LLM or embedding call - unit test with a spy client (`test_rejected_input_makes_no_api_call`, `test_rate_limited_input_makes_no_api_call`, with `test_accepted_input_reaches_the_clients` as the control).
**Notes:** the attack strings are written by us, not copied from other projects with unknown licences. The rate limit counts accepted questions (one per turn), and rejected input does not count (owner's choice, 2026-10-19). T3.1 wires `run_guarded()` into the chat turn.

### T1.4 Security reviewer agent (S) · status: done · issue #32
**Serves:** beyond the brief (owner's request); supports core 3 (security measures relevant to the domain) and Medium 3 (prompt injection protection).
**Depends on:** T1.1
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md` exists, with the same frontmatter fields and style as `.claude/agents/code-reviewer.md` - checked by the code-reviewer agent.
- [x] The agent maps every path from user input, retrieved text and tool calls to the LLM, tools and UI, and for each surface (direct prompt injection, indirect injection through retrieved text, model-produced tool arguments, Markdown/HTML/link output injection, Chroma metadata/filter injection, path and command injection in scripts, guard before spend, key handling, personal-data leaks) checks the existing controls against `docs/SECURITY.md` and says what an attacker could still do - checked by reading the agent file (Step 1 and Step 2).
- [x] Safety rules: read-only for the repo; never opens `.streamlit/secrets.toml` or `.env`, never prints a key, never calls OpenRouter or any other service (its only network use is GitHub, through `git` and `gh`); its own attack cases run only as offline tests with mocked LLM and embeddings in a scratch directory outside the repo and are never committed; new test cases are only proposed in its report - checked by reading the agent file (Ground rules, Step 3) and by `git status` showing no change after the run against `main`.
- [x] The report gives each finding a severity, `path:line`, a concrete attack string or scenario, the impact and the smallest fix, plus a list of controls that held - checked by one run against `main` (recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md`).
- [x] `CLAUDE.md` ("Where things are") and `docs/SECURITY.md` name the agent - checked by reading them.
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-20): read-only (tests are only proposed), never a live run against the real model, optional tool (not added to `.claude/rules/workflow.md`); criterion 3 reworded to allow GitHub through `git`/`gh` (owner's choice in the PR #39 review). Attack strings are our own, not copied from other projects.

### T1.5 Docs: run commands use the project's venv (S) · status: done · issue #42
**Serves:** the evaluation's technical item "the app works (you can chat and get answers)" (`docs/BRIEF.md`), when run as documented; prepares T6.2 (README).
**Depends on:** T1.1
**Acceptance criteria** (approved by the owner, 2026-10-20)
- [x] Right before the run commands, the README says they must run in a terminal with `.venv` active (`(.venv)` at the start of the prompt), gives the way without activating (`.venv\Scripts\streamlit.exe run app.py` on Windows, `.venv/bin/streamlit run app.py` on macOS and Linux), and names the symptom of the wrong Python (`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'langchain_chroma'`) - review.
- [x] The README lists the steps before the first question: set the key, `python scripts/download_sources.py`, `python scripts/build_index.py`, then run the app, with the note to stop the app before rebuilding the index - review.
- [x] The "Run" line in `CLAUDE.md` says to use the venv too - review.
- [x] The documented command `.venv\Scripts\streamlit.exe run app.py` is run in a new terminal and the app loads without error; CI passes - manual check done (2026-10-20: typed as written in a new PowerShell tab; the app loaded with its title and four tabs and no error; local ruff and pytest pass); CI green on PR #43, merged 2026-10-20.
**Notes:** found on 2026-10-20 when the owner started the app with the system Python's `streamlit` (1.64, without the project's packages) after PR #41 merged.

### T1.6 CI: accept the pillow 12.3.0 advisories until Streamlit allows pillow 13 (S) · status: done · issue #55
**Serves:** core 3 and 4 (security measures, error-free CI with a dependency audit); `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md` (no ignore without a documented reason). Owner's request (2026-10-20), after CI's security job failed on PR #53 (T4.7) and on every branch.
**Depends on:** T1.1
**Acceptance criteria** (from the owner's request, 2026-10-20)
- [x] `.github/workflows/ci.yml` ignores exactly the eight pillow advisories by ID, with a comment giving the reason and when to remove them; the chromadb ignores are unchanged - review, and `pip-audit .` with the CI flags passes locally (2026-10-20: "No known vulnerabilities found, 13 ignored" for the 12 IDs).
- [x] `docs/SECURITY.md` lists each advisory under "Accepted risks" (ID, GHSA, what it is, why it does not apply here, when to review), and says Pillow is not used by the app's code - review; `tests/test_ci_audit.py`: `test_every_ignored_advisory_has_an_accepted_risk_row`, `test_no_advisory_is_ignored_twice`, `test_ci_ignores_some_advisories`.
- [x] CI's security job passes on the PR - CI green on PR #58 (lint, test, security and linked-issue passed on 269d0cb, 2026-10-20).
**Notes:** pillow 13.0.0 fixes all eight; streamlit 1.66.0 requires `pillow<13` and no later streamlit exists (checked on PyPI, 2026-10-20). Remove the ignores when Dependabot proposes a streamlit release that allows pillow 13 (done in T1.6a, #77). If a later ticket adds an image upload or reads images with Pillow, or if the PDF export (T5.2) draws images or lets text reach reportlab markup unescaped (an `<img src>` tag makes reportlab fetch and open the image with Pillow), review these ignores first. Code review on PR #58 (ready to merge): this trigger made explicit, and the test matches whole IDs and the `--ignore-vuln=ID` form. PR #58 merged before these fixes were pushed, so they reached `main` in a follow-up PR (#62). Its third fix, writing T5.2 in `docs/SECURITY.md` as planned, was dropped when `main` was merged into #62: T5.2 had shipped by then (#54), so the present tense is right.

### T1.6a CI: upgrade streamlit to 1.66.1 so pillow 13 replaces the pillow ignores (S) · status: done · issue #77

### T1.8 A fixed message for unexpected errors in a chat turn (S) · status: done · issue #82
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): core 4 (proper error handling, real-world use), evaluation: core concepts (error scenarios and edge cases) and technical (appropriate security: no error text on the page), Sprint 2 review point 1 in part (an unexpected error leaves a log line); Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): core 4 (proper error handling), Medium 10 in part (logging), evaluation: core concepts (error scenarios and edge cases) and technical (appropriate security); AC-13. Finding 2 of the 2026-10-22 audit, second half; split from T1.7 by the owner (2026-10-22), to follow after the course submission.
**Depends on:** T1.7
**Acceptance criteria** (approved by the owner, 2026-10-22)
- [x] Any other `Exception` raised after the guard accepts a question (opening the index, building the models, query translation, retrieval, the answer, the tools) makes `chat.run_turn` return an `"error"` result with a new fixed message (code `turn.error`, English and German in `src/i18n.py`; the owner reviews the German text), the steps run and the tokens spent so far; the original is not chained or shown - `tests/test_chat.py`: a fake that raises `RuntimeError("SECRET-MARKER")` at each of these points; "SECRET-MARKER" appears nowhere in the result. Done: `test_an_unexpected_error_gives_the_fixed_turn_error` (seven points: opening the index, building each model, query translation, the vector search, the answer call, building the tools; the steps per point; the marker not in the result's `repr`), `test_an_unexpected_error_keeps_the_tokens_spent_before_it`, `test_the_turn_error_is_shown_in_the_chosen_language`; the English and German texts approved by the owner on 2026-10-25. `tests/conftest.py` fails any other test whose turn ends in `turn.error`, so the catch-all cannot hide a fake's assertion or a regression (tests that expect it carry the `turn_error` marker).
- [x] The catch-all writes one log line with the exception's type and where it was raised (as `LLMError.diagnostic`), never its message or arguments - `tests/test_chat.py` with `caplog`. Done: `test_an_unexpected_error_logs_its_type_and_place_only` (one ERROR record from `src.chat` at each point, no `exc_info`, "RuntimeError in ...", no marker), and `test_a_named_error_writes_no_catch_all_line`.
- [x] Streamlit's control exceptions (`RerunException`, `StopException`, which derive from `BaseException`) are not caught - a test with a `BaseException` raised inside the turn. Done: `test_a_base_exception_inside_the_turn_is_not_caught`.
- [x] An unknown answer language still raises `ValueError` before any call, and a rejected question still makes no API call - the existing tests keep passing. Done: the check sits before the `try`, and the existing tests pass.
- [x] In the app, such an error shows the fixed message in the chosen language, no "SECRET-MARKER", and the chat history stays - AppTest in `tests/test_app_chat.py`. Done: `test_an_unexpected_error_shows_the_fixed_message_in_the_chosen_language` (English, then German after the switch).
- [x] `docs/SECURITY.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 list the catch-all - review.
- [x] `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .` and `python -m pytest -q` pass - CI.
- [x] Records: this plan (T1.8), `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-22): a new message rather than `llm.error` (which names the model service); log only the type and location. Plan drafted in the T1.7 session: make `llm._diagnostic` public (now `llm.error_diagnostic()`), put `TURN_ERROR_MESSAGE` in `src/config.py`, add `except Exception` after the named handlers in `run_turn()`. A catch-all can hide a UI bug raised in the app's `on_text`/`on_step` callbacks in AppTests; the tests that check the answer's content still catch it.

- [x] The README has a copyright notice for the project's own code and documentation: Copyright (c) 2026 Nguyen Mai Hoang, all rights reserved; the code may be viewed and run to evaluate it, and any other use (copying, modifying, redistributing, commercial use) needs the author's written permission; third-party content keeps its own licence and is not covered by the notice (links to `docs/SOURCES.md`, `ATTRIBUTION.md` and `assets/fonts/LICENSE`) - review: `README.md`, "Copyright and licences".
- [x] There is no `LICENSE` file at the repo root, and `pyproject.toml` declares no open-source licence (no `license` field and no licence classifier) - review: both were already true; nothing changed.
- [x] `docs/PRD.md` records the decision: the open item "The repo's own code licence" is closed with the owner's choice and date, and "possibly opened as open source later" in "Beyond the brief" is reworded to match - review.
- [x] `.claude/rules/copyright.md` matches the decision: the intro no longer says the repo may be opened as open source, and says that third-party content must still be reusable and redistributable by anyone while the owner's own code is all rights reserved; rule 9's "The repo's own code licence (if one is added)" is reworded to match; nothing else in the policy changes - review.
- [x] `.claude/agents/compliance-reviewer.md` item 10 no longer reports the missing code licence as an open decision: it checks that the README notice is there and that no open-source licence was added without the owner's decision - review.
- [ ] The `compliance-reviewer` agent runs on the PR with no open Blocker or High finding, and its report is on the PR - the PR's comments.
- [x] T1.14 is in this plan, and a `docs/PROGRESS.md` entry records the owner's decision - review.
- [ ] CI passes - CI on the PR.
**Notes:** docs and agent instructions only, no code. The notice only states the default (without a licence nobody may reuse the code anyway), but it makes the owner's intent plain; it cannot stop anyone from viewing or cloning a public repository, and GitHub's terms let other users view and fork it on GitHub. Left as it is (owner's choice, 2026-11-09): `ATTRIBUTION.md`'s line that "the licence of this repository's code does not apply to them", generated by `src/rag/sources.py`; it stays true for the all-rights-reserved terms, and rewording it would change code and the generated file. Also in this PR (owner's choice): T1.13 recorded as done. Owner check, not part of this ticket: whether Turing College's terms grant the school rights over work copied into the course repository (T9.11b).

- [x] The real index is built once - owner live check (O5), with chunk count and cost recorded in `docs/TESTING.md` (run by the owner on 2026-10-19: 1,810 chunks from 21 sources; cost estimated at about $0.003 from the character count, since langchain does not return the token use of embeddings and OpenRouter's activity chart shows only daily totals, under $0.01 that day; owner's choice to record the estimate).
**Notes:** a build writes a staging collection and renames it only when complete, so a failed build keeps the old index; the collection records the embedding model, and an index from another model is refused. The build embeds approved source text, not user input, so the input guard does not apply to it.

Until T3.3, the lists are merged by keeping each chunk once at its smallest distance, nearest first. A filter that leaves nothing within `RETRIEVAL_MAX_DISTANCE` is dropped; a filter that leaves fewer but relevant chunks is kept, so the prompt (O7) should set filters only when the question asks for them. The rewrites go only to the search and the view, never to the answer model.

The single letters still had 1-4 chunks within the limit; T3.8 made only the question's own hits decide coverage, and the owner kept the remaining case (a very short question typed by the user) as a known limit, with no ticket of its own (T3.8's entry in `docs/PROGRESS.md`).
**Notes:** found in T3.5's live check (2026-10-21): tiny chunks such as `Abstract > Clinical Trial Registration` with only a PROSPERO ID (64 characters, #14) were within 0.62 of the queries "A", "B" and "C" (nearest 0.550-0.596). Offline count on the 21 articles: 125 of 1,810 chunks under 300 characters; 40 are the last piece of a longer section and 65 a first or middle piece (both mostly the overlap repeated from the chunk before: median 77 and 58 new characters); 20 are a whole section, of which 3 are registration boilerplate and 17 real content (abstract objectives and conclusions, results subsections), which stay. Short chunks are not central in the embedding space (only 1 among the 100 chunks most similar to all others), so they match short queries because both are short; removing them cannot guarantee that a one-letter query finds nothing, hence the live check. Owner's decision (2026-10-21): options A (drop the registration sections, by title, since they have no `sec-type`) and B (merge short pieces into a neighbouring chunk: first the last piece only; after the implementation showed 64 short first and middle pieces left, the owner extended it to them, capped at `CHUNK_SIZE + CHUNK_MIN_CHARS`) of: a minimum chunk length (would drop real content), merging abstract subsections (would span sections), keeping the section title out of the embedded text (would change every embedding and T2.2's criterion), or a stricter distance for rewrites (`RETRIEVAL_MAX_DISTANCE` was calibrated on questions only; left until the live check's numbers). Known limit (PR #69 review): the pieces are placed in the text by the code (the last one from the end); a middle piece whose words repeat exactly where two pieces overlap could be placed at the earlier copy; the pieces then either stay as split (a short piece may remain) or are joined at slightly shifted positions, and in both cases no text is lost or repeated (PR #69 review round 2). None of the 489 sections of the 21 articles hits this.

### T3.8 Only the question itself decides whether the sources cover it (S) · status: done · issue #71
**Serves:** core 1 (RAG with query translation); AC-2. Refines T3.2 and T3.3; makes T3.5's skip of the answer call apply (cost control, beyond the brief).
**Depends on:** T3.2, T3.3, T3.5 (PR #67 merged first; the branch takes `main` after it), O5 (the owner's live re-check).
**Acceptance criteria** (approved by the owner on 2026-10-21)
- [x] A rewrite or a keyword match alone never makes a question covered: with no chunk within `RETRIEVAL_MAX_DISTANCE` of the question itself (with the filter, or without it after the retry), the turn has no relevant chunk even when a rewrite has one; with `needs_tool` false no answer call is made, the search details are kept - integration tests on the fixture index (all embedding and model calls faked): `tests/test_chat.py::test_rewrites_alone_do_not_make_a_question_covered`, `test_with_only_a_rewrite_close_and_no_tool_no_answer_call_is_made`, `test_a_chunk_found_only_by_a_rewrite_does_not_reach_the_model` (T3.2's review test turned round); T3.3's `test_a_keyword_match_alone_does_not_make_a_question_covered` still passes. All new tests fail with the old gate (checked).
- [x] When the question itself is covered, the rewrites and keyword search still add chunks the question alone misses (T3.2, T3.3) - integration tests (T3.2's `test_a_rewrite_finds_chunks_the_question_alone_misses` rewritten for a covered question: the rewrite's chunk carries a distance, so vector search found it; `test_an_exact_term_finds_the_hmb_source_that_vector_search_misses` unchanged; both pass).
- [x] With no relevant chunk and `needs_tool` true, the tools-only path of T3.5 runs as before - integration test (`test_with_only_a_rewrite_close_and_needs_tool_the_tools_only_call_runs`: one call with the tools, `NO_SOURCES_BLOCK` and none of the rewrites' chunks).
- [x] The filter retry decides coverage on the question the same way: a filter under which only a rewrite has a hit makes the search run again without it - integration test (`test_a_filter_under_which_only_a_rewrite_has_a_hit_is_dropped`).
- [x] `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .` and `python -m pytest -q` pass; `scripts/compare_retrieval.py` and `scripts/live_check_retrieval.py` are unchanged in behaviour (their tests pass) - local run 2026-10-21: 2,517 passed; CI on the PR.
- [x] Owner live check (O5) on the real index (done 2026-10-21, `docs/TESTING.md`: offside 3 of 3 stopped at about 3,600 tokens and $0.0001 each, the three covered questions answered with sources; the record-only questions within the limit on their own, 0.343-0.527). **Pass:** the offside question asked 3 times in the app ends 3 of 3 as "No matching research found" with no answer round, and covered questions of the T3.1 calibration and T3.2's live check (e.g. "How much protein per meal?", "What should I eat to bulk?", the ISSN caffeine question) are still answered with `[S#]` sources. **Recorded, not pass or fail** (owner's choice after the PR #72 review, 2026-10-21): vague questions never measured on the real index ("bulk diet?", "What should I eat late at night?") and one or two covered questions in German ("Wie viel Protein pro Mahlzeit?"; the query translation writes English rewrites, which before T3.8 made a German question covered, while now the German wording itself must come within a limit calibrated on English questions); a rejection there is input for T7.2 or a ticket of its own. Results recorded in `docs/TESTING.md`.
- [x] Docs: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 5 (rewrites widen the search but do not decide coverage), `docs/SECURITY.md` ("Answers without evidence" and the known limit for single-letter questions), `src/config.py`'s comment on the limit, this plan, `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
**Notes:** found in T3.5's live check after T3.7 (2026-10-21, real index, 1,703 chunks): "What is the offside rule in football?" reached the answer model in 2 of 3 app runs. In one captured run the question and two rewrites had 0 of 6 chunks within 0.62 (nearest 0.662-0.693), but the rewrite "VAR and offside rule in football" had 6 of 6 (0.548-0.592), and `hybrid_search()` counted a question as covered when any query had a chunk within the limit, so T3.5's skip never applied. The limit was calibrated on questions as asked (T3.1, 28 questions: covered at most 0.542, unrelated at least 0.700), never on rewrites. Owner's decision (2026-10-21): option 1 of (1) the question itself must have a chunk within the limit, (2) a stricter limit for rewrites (would need to be under 0.548, from one observation), (3) a chunk counts only within the limit of the question or of at least two queries (keeps T3.2's vague case, but rewrites stay uncalibrated), (4) a prompt rule against acronyms (fragile). A looser limit for the question is not usable: the offside question itself is at 0.659-0.665. What option 1 loses: a vague question whose own wording is further than 0.62 while its rewrites are close now gets "no matching research" (a question a tool could answer still takes T3.5's `needs_tool` path). Single-letter questions typed by the user ("A", "B", "C" sit within 0.62 themselves; the guard has no minimum length) are out of scope and recorded as a known limit (T3.7's last criterion left them to a ticket of their own; owner's choice, 2026-10-21).

### T3.4 Prompt injection hardening (S) · status: done · issue #10
**Serves:** Medium 3; AC-11.
**Depends on:** T3.1
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] User text is sent only inside `<user_input>` in the user message, and retrieved text only inside `<sources>`; neither is ever concatenated into the system prompt - unit test on the built messages (`tests/test_prompt_injection.py`: `test_a_look_alike_in_the_question_stays_inside_user_input`, `test_a_look_alike_in_chunk_text_stays_inside_sources`, which check that the four delimiters are the only tags, in order, and that the system message is the prompt alone; `tests/test_chat.py::test_the_question_and_the_chunks_are_delimited_in_the_user_message`).
- [x] A retrieved chunk containing an instruction ("ignore previous instructions...") does not change the system prompt or tool use in the built request, and the system prompt tells the model to treat both blocks as data - unit test with a fixture chunk (`test_an_injected_chunk_does_not_change_the_system_prompt_or_tool_use`: a turn on a fixture index with a made-up injected chunk sends the same system prompt and request shape as a clean one, with no tool calls, and the chunk only escaped inside `<sources>`; `test_the_answer_prompt_tells_the_model_both_blocks_are_data` on the owner's prompt).
- [x] LLM output is rendered without `unsafe_allow_html` anywhere in the app - test scanning the app source, as in Sprint 1 (`test_nothing_in_the_app_renders_with_unsafe_allow_html`: an AST scan of `app.py` and `src/`, so comments do not count, for `unsafe_allow_html` and `unsafe_allow_javascript`; `test_the_app_uses_no_raw_html_components`).
- [x] User text, chunk text and any chunk metadata put in the prompt are escaped before they are wrapped, so a delimiter look-alike (`</user_input>`, `</user-input>`, `<user_input id="2">`, `</sources lang="en">`, `<sources/>`) cannot close or open a block and a `**System:**` or `> System:` line stays data; user text is passed as message content, never as prompt-template text - parametrised unit test on the built messages (follow-up from the T1.4 security audit, finding 3) (the three `test_a_look_alike_in_...` tests over seven look-alikes each, in the question, the chunk text and the title and year; `test_the_escape_keeps_the_text_exact_and_marks_typed_entities`, `test_braces_in_the_question_are_passed_as_text_not_as_a_template`, `test_no_prompt_template_is_used_in_the_app`).
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-20): the guard's patterns are widened too (attributes, `-` or a space in "user input", a closing slash, `<source>`, role lines after `**`, `>` or `_`), with new attack and benign strings and a speed test in `tests/test_guard.py`; the answer prompt is not changed (it already says both blocks are data, and models read `&lt;` as `<`). Escaping is `html.escape` without quotes (`escape_prompt_data()` in `src/prompt_data.py`, a module that imports nothing from `src`, so T3.2's query translation in `src/rag/` and T4.4's tool loop can use it without an import cycle; `test_the_escape_module_imports_nothing_from_src`), after folding full-width and small-form brackets (`test_full_width_and_small_form_brackets_in_a_chunk_are_escaped`). After code review round 1 the guard's tag pattern only counts attribute-shaped text, so comparisons such as "(<sources say 25%) and > 1200 kcal" pass, and the AC3 scan also flags `unsafe_allow_html` passed by position, `*` or `**` (`test_the_render_scan_finds_every_way_to_allow_html`).

Since 2026-10-21 the prompt also names the full-day meal plan check that criterion 1 counts since T4.5.
- [x] `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md` and `docs/PROGRESS.md` say that an off-topic question makes no answer call again (done); owner live check (O5, two runs on 2026-10-21, `docs/TESTING.md`): "How many kcal are in 150 g of rolled oats?" is still answered from the food lookup (both runs), and "What is the offside rule in football?" made no answer model call in the first run ("No matching research found"); in the second run a short rewrite matched boilerplate chunks within the distance limit, so the question took the "has sources" path and the model was called. The owner counts the criterion as met with that finding recorded (2026-10-21): T3.5's skip works when nothing is relevant, and the boilerplate chunks go to a separate ticket (T3.7, done). After T3.7 (same day, `docs/TESTING.md`) the offside question still reached the answer call in 2 of 3 app runs, through one rewrite alone ("VAR and offside rule in football", 6 of 6 chunks within the limit while the question had none), as the limit is checked for each rewrite on its own: a cost gap, not a safety gap; the owner accepted it, and a follow-up retrieval ticket (T3.8) deals with it.

### T4.2 Energy and macro tools with the calorie floor (M) · status: done · issue #12
**Serves:** core 2 (tools 1 and 2); AC-7.
**Depends on:** T4.1, O3
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `calculate_tdee` returns BMR and TDEE (Mifflin-St Jeor, activity factors from config) - known-answer unit tests (`tests/test_energy.py`: `test_tdee_known_answers` (five profiles, both sexes, every activity level), `test_tdee_uses_the_unrounded_bmr`, `test_each_activity_level_multiplies_the_same_bmr`, `test_config_holds_the_source_values`, `test_every_profile_option_has_a_factor_and_a_protein_target`, `test_tdee_names_its_method_and_sources`, `test_activity_labels_name_the_frequencies_of_the_factor_source`).
- [x] `calculate_macro_targets` returns kcal, protein, carbohydrate and fat (g and %) for each goal, using the ranges approved in O3 with their source IDs in comments - unit tests (`test_maintain_known_answer`, `test_lose_fat_known_answer`, `test_build_muscle_known_answer`, `test_targets_sit_inside_the_source_ranges`, `test_macro_targets_cite_the_approved_sources`, `test_macro_targets_chain_from_calculate_tdee`, `test_protein_is_lowered_when_it_does_not_fit_the_target`; source IDs in the comments of the energy block in `src/config.py`).
- [x] No goal or profile produces a calorie target below the calorie floor (AC-7) - parametrised unit tests over extreme profiles (`test_no_profile_gets_a_target_below_the_calorie_floor`: every goal over 450 profiles, the corners of every range included, checking the target, the range and the macro grams; `test_the_lowest_tdees_are_raised_to_the_floor`, `test_a_target_below_the_floor_is_raised_and_noted`, `test_a_floor_above_the_needs_says_it_will_not_lead_to_fat_loss`, `test_a_floor_equal_to_the_needs_says_it_will_not_lead_to_fat_loss`, `test_a_floor_above_the_needs_is_noted_for_the_other_goals`, `test_a_target_at_the_floor_is_not_marked_as_raised`).
- [x] Tool arguments are validated with Pydantic ranges; invalid input returns a clear error, not an exception - unit tests (`test_invalid_tdee_arguments_give_an_error_result`, `test_invalid_macro_arguments_give_an_error_result`: out of range, strings, booleans, NaN, infinity, None, unknown options; `test_every_bad_tdee_argument_is_reported_at_once`, `test_invalid_messages_do_not_echo_the_input`, `test_the_argument_schemas_carry_the_ranges`, `test_the_tdee_range_covers_every_profile`).
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-19, O3 and copyright rule 4): two new formula sources, #22 (Mifflin-St Jeor coefficients) and #23 (activity factors), CC BY, licence read on the publisher pages, credited in `ATTRIBUTION.md`; the activity labels now follow #23 (times a week; moderate is 4-5); calorie floor 1200 kcal/day for everyone (top of the low-energy diet range in #1 and #9); lose fat -0.5% of body weight a week at about 3500 kcal per lb (#1, #12); build muscle +10% (#11); protein 2.2 g/kg for fat loss and 1.9 otherwise (range 1.6-2.2, #1, #11); fat 25% of kcal (#11, #12); carbohydrate the rest (#11); when protein and fat do not fit (very heavy body, low target), protein is lowered and carbohydrate is 0, with a note. The LangChain wrappers and reading the profile from the session are T4.4's.

### T4.4 Tool-calling loop and tool results UI (M) · status: done · issue #14
**Serves:** core 2 and 5; Medium 6; AC-6.
**Depends on:** T3.1, T4.2, T4.3
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] The answer step binds the tools and runs at most 4 tool rounds; a tool error returns to the model as a message - integration tests with a scripted fake model in `tests/test_tool_loop.py`: one tool (`test_one_tool_call_runs_and_the_model_answers_from_its_result`, `test_every_call_binds_the_three_tools`), two tools (`test_two_tools_in_one_round_both_run_in_order`, `test_rounds_follow_each_other_and_the_steps_show_them`), an error (`test_a_tool_error_returns_to_the_model_as_a_message`, `test_invalid_arguments_return_to_the_model_as_a_message`, `test_arguments_that_are_not_json_get_the_fixed_message`), the round limit (`test_the_loop_stops_after_four_rounds_and_asks_for_the_answer`, `test_tool_calls_after_the_last_round_end_the_turn_with_an_error`), plus `test_at_most_six_calls_of_one_reply_run` and `test_a_cut_off_tool_call_is_not_run`; on the wire with the real `ChatOpenAI` and a fake streaming server: `test_the_real_client_sends_the_tools_and_the_tool_round`, `test_the_real_client_sends_tool_choice_none_after_the_last_round`.
- [x] Energy tools read the profile from the session; without a profile they return "profile needed" and the UI points to the form - unit tests (`tests/test_tools_registry.py`: `test_without_a_profile_the_energy_tools_say_profile_needed`, `test_the_energy_needs_come_from_the_profile`, `test_the_macro_targets_use_the_profiles_goal_and_energy_needs`; `tests/test_tool_loop.py`: `test_the_energy_tools_use_the_profile_of_the_turn`, `test_without_a_profile_an_energy_tool_returns_profile_needed`) and AppTest (`tests/test_app_tools.py`: `test_the_energy_tools_read_the_profile_from_the_session`, `test_without_a_profile_the_result_points_to_the_form`: the "Add your details" button in the result opens the dialog).
- [x] Each tool result appears in a "Tool results" expander as a table; macro targets also as a chart (AC-6) - AppTest (`test_each_tool_result_appears_in_the_tool_results_expander_as_a_table`, `test_macro_targets_also_appear_as_a_chart_in_the_macro_colours`, `test_a_food_lookup_alone_draws_no_chart`, `test_tool_results_stay_with_their_answer_in_the_history`) and a manual browser check with a fake model (screenshot `docs/ui/t4.4-tool-results.jpg`).
- [x] Each tool is built with an explicit `args_schema` that holds only the model's fields: `lookup_food_nutrition` takes only `food` and `grams` (never `table`), the energy tools take no profile fields; `FoodLookupArgs` has `extra="forbid"`; a call with `table`, a profile field or any extra field is refused with the fixed message and runs no lookup - unit test on the built tools (`test_each_tool_is_built_with_an_explicit_schema_of_the_models_fields`, `test_the_schemas_refuse_any_extra_field`, `test_what_the_model_sees_has_no_table_and_no_profile_field`, `test_a_call_with_an_extra_field_is_refused_and_runs_nothing` (spies fail if a tool runs), `test_the_extra_field_message_is_fixed`, `test_a_call_through_langchain_is_refused_too`) (follow-up from the T1.4 security audit, finding 2).
- [x] USDA names in a tool result (`matched_name`, `alternatives`, `message`) reach the model only inside the tool result and appear in the UI as plain text, so a name with Markdown or a delimiter cannot add a link, an image or a block - unit tests (`test_usda_names_are_returned_inside_the_tool_message_as_data`, `test_a_usda_name_reaches_the_model_only_inside_the_tool_message`) and AppTest with a fixture table (`test_usda_names_appear_only_as_plain_text`, the name as the match and as an alternative; it failed when a caption showed the found message, checked once) (follow-up from the T1.4 security audit, finding 4).
- [x] An instruction in a retrieved chunk does not change the tools bound or `tool_choice`: a clean and an injected turn pass the same tool arguments to the model - `tests/test_prompt_injection.py::test_an_injected_chunk_does_not_change_the_system_prompt_or_tool_use` now compares `ScriptedChatModel.call_kwargs` with the tools bound: the same three tools and `tool_choice` None in both turns (follow-up from T3.4; owner's choice, 2026-10-20).
- [x] The macro bar, the targets donut and the tool result table are readable in light and dark mode - manual check in both modes, recorded in `docs/TESTING.md` (2026-10-20, fake model, sample profile; screenshots `docs/ui/t4.4-tool-results-light.jpg` and `docs/ui/t4.4-tool-results-dark.jpg`): tables, legend, axis and captions readable in both. Measured against the background, the carbohydrate and fat bars are 2.0:1 and 2.2:1 in light mode and protein 3.0:1 in dark mode, below or at WCAG's 3:1 for graphics; the values are in the table above the bar, so colour is not the only signal. The targets donut is T4.7's (split from T4.4), checked there (moved from T7.3; owner's choice, 2026-10-20). Beyond the brief (owner's request).
- [x] The profile summary and tool results that go into the prompt are escaped with `escape_prompt_data()` and sit only in their own delimited block or tool message, never in the system prompt, so a look-alike in a USDA name cannot close or open a block - parametrised unit tests on the built messages: `test_the_profile_block_is_escaped_and_stays_in_its_block` and `test_a_look_alike_in_a_usda_name_stays_inside_the_tool_message` (every look-alike of T3.4), plus `test_usda_names_are_returned_inside_the_tool_message_as_data` and `test_a_usda_name_reaches_the_model_only_inside_the_tool_message` (follow-up from T3.4; owner's choice, 2026-10-20).
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-20): the sidebar daily target and My targets card, the My targets tab and the Food lookup tab moved to T4.7, so this ticket keeps the tools in the chat; one "Tool results (n)" expander, like "Sources (n)"; the profile is sent to the model in a `<profile>` block of the user message; after 4 tool rounds one more call with `tool_choice="none"` writes the answer; when no chunk is within the distance limit, the model is still called once with the tools, its first reply hidden, and without a tool call the app shows `NO_SOURCES_MESSAGE` (B2, after the owner's live check in `docs/TESTING.md`; AC-2 holds, but that path now makes one model call) - `test_with_no_source_a_tool_can_still_answer`, `test_with_no_source_and_no_tool_the_fixed_message_is_shown`, `test_with_no_relevant_chunk_the_app_says_the_sources_do_not_cover_it`, `test_no_relevant_source_shows_the_fixed_message_and_no_sources` (T3.5: that call is now made only when the query plan says `needs_tool`; those two tests now check that an off-topic question makes no answer call, and T4.4's one hidden call is checked by `test_with_no_relevant_chunk_a_tool_answerable_question_gets_one_unseen_call` and `test_with_no_source_a_tool_answerable_question_calls_the_model_unseen`). LangChain skips argument validation for a schema without fields, so `run_tool_call()` validates every call against its schema itself. The system prompt's new sections were drafted by the owner and edited after review with the owner's approval (O1); `test_the_answer_prompt_matches_the_code` checks it names `<profile>`, `matched_name` and `profile_needed`. `tests/fakes.py::ScriptedChatModel` must implement `bind_tools` (LangChain's `BaseChatModel.bind_tools` raises `NotImplementedError`) and keep recording the bound tools in `call_kwargs`, so the tool-use check above compares real arguments (PR #45 review, 2026-10-20). Import `escape_prompt_data()` from `src/prompt_data.py`.

### T4.5 `evaluate_meal_plan` tool (stretch) (S) · status: done · issue #15
**Serves:** core 2 (extra tool: the brief asks for at least 3, this is the 4th). Only if ahead of schedule; done before the remaining core tickets (T3.3, T3.5, T4.7, T6.1) by the owner's choice (2026-10-20).
**Depends on:** T4.4
**Acceptance criteria** (approved by the owner, 2026-10-20; the first draft had only "totals for a list of foods and amounts are compared with the targets, with the gaps listed - unit tests")
- [x] `evaluate_meal_plan` takes `items`: 1 to `MEAL_PLAN_MAX_ITEMS` (20) entries of `{food, grams}`, each checked with the food lookup's rules; any other field is refused (`extra="forbid"` on the list and on each item); the targets come from the session's profile (the model can send no target and no body data); without a profile the result is `profile_needed` - `tests/test_tools_registry.py` (`test_each_tool_is_built_with_an_explicit_schema_of_the_models_fields`, `test_the_schemas_refuse_any_extra_field`, `test_the_meal_plans_items_hold_only_food_and_grams`, `test_a_call_with_an_extra_field_is_refused_and_runs_nothing`, `test_what_the_model_sees_has_no_table_and_no_profile_field`, `test_without_a_profile_the_profile_tools_say_profile_needed`, `test_the_meal_plan_compares_with_the_profiles_daily_targets`).
- [x] Each food is looked up as by `lookup_food_nutrition` (USDA match, kcal, protein, carbohydrate, fat, fiber); the totals add up only the foods found; foods not found are listed with their suggestions and the result says the totals are partial - `tests/test_meal_plan.py` (`test_each_food_is_looked_up_with_its_match_and_nutrients`, `test_the_totals_add_up_the_foods_as_shown`, `test_the_fiber_total_leaves_out_foods_without_a_fiber_value`, `test_without_any_fiber_value_the_fiber_total_is_not_reported`, `test_a_food_not_found_is_listed_and_left_out_of_the_totals`, `test_when_no_food_is_found_there_are_no_totals`, `test_alternatives_come_back_for_each_food`).
- [x] Against `calculate_macro_targets`' daily targets: each of kcal, protein, carbohydrate and fat has its total, target and gap (target minus total: positive below, negative above); kcal is placed against `kcal_range` and protein against `protein_g_range` (below, within, above; both from the approved sources); carbohydrate and fat get only the gap (no range from the sources, none invented); fiber has a total only (no target) - `tests/test_meal_plan.py` (`test_each_gap_is_the_target_minus_the_plans_total`, `test_a_plan_above_the_target_has_a_negative_gap`, `test_the_kcal_total_is_placed_against_the_goals_range`, `test_the_protein_total_is_placed_against_the_sources_range`, `test_the_ends_of_a_range_are_within_it`, `test_carbohydrate_and_fat_have_no_range_and_fiber_no_target`).
- [x] A plan whose kcal total is below the calorie floor (1200 kcal/day) gets a note saying so, which also points to a registered dietitian (added by the owner after PR #56's review, 2026-10-20); the targets' own notes (floor applied, protein lowered) are passed on - `tests/test_meal_plan.py` (`test_a_plan_below_the_calorie_floor_says_so`, `test_a_plan_at_or_above_the_floor_has_no_floor_note`, `test_the_floor_is_read_from_config`, `test_the_targets_notes_are_passed_on`).
- [x] The model can call the tool from the chat; its result appears in "Tool results" as tables (the foods; the totals against the targets); the names asked for and the USDA names are shown as plain text (`st.dataframe`) - `tests/test_tool_loop.py` (`test_every_call_binds_the_four_tools`, `test_a_meal_plan_call_runs_and_its_result_reaches_the_model`, `test_a_usda_name_in_a_meal_plan_reaches_the_model_only_inside_the_tool_message`), `tests/test_app_tools.py` (`test_a_meal_plan_shows_its_foods_and_the_totals_against_the_targets`, `test_a_meal_plan_above_the_target_says_so`, `test_a_meal_plan_with_no_food_found_shows_only_the_foods`, `test_a_meal_plan_without_a_profile_points_to_the_form`, `test_meal_plan_names_appear_only_as_plain_text`); browser check with a fake model (`docs/TESTING.md`, screenshot `docs/ui/t4.5-meal-plan-check.jpg`).
- [x] Invalid arguments, too many foods and a broken foods table give a fixed message that does not repeat what was sent - `tests/test_meal_plan.py` (`test_invalid_items_are_refused_before_any_lookup`, `test_the_invalid_message_names_the_items_and_never_echoes_them`, `test_a_list_problem_gets_the_list_rule`, `test_the_limit_of_foods_is_accepted`), `tests/test_tools_registry.py` (`test_invalid_meal_plan_arguments_give_the_meal_plan_message`, `test_a_missing_foods_table_gives_the_fixed_tool_error`), `tests/test_app_tools.py` (`test_an_invalid_meal_plan_shows_its_fixed_message`).
- [x] At most `MAX_MEAL_PLAN_CALLS_PER_ROUND` (1) meal plan check runs from one reply; the others get the fixed "Not run" message, the reply's other tools still run, and the next round may call it again; the measured worst case is in `docs/SECURITY.md` (added by the owner after code review, 2026-10-20) - `tests/test_tool_loop.py` (`test_one_meal_plan_runs_per_reply_and_the_rest_get_the_fixed_message`, `test_the_meal_plan_limit_is_per_round`).
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-20): tables only, no chart; code first, then the owner's answer prompt lines on the tool (O1, done; `test_the_answer_prompt_matches_the_code` now looks for `evaluate_meal_plan`). A meal plan is one day's foods, compared with the daily targets; amounts in grams only. The result keeps the name asked for (`requested`), shown only as a table cell and escaped in the tool message like the USDA names. The totals add the values shown per food, so the table adds up.

### T4.6 Profile form: a hint instead of "Press Enter to apply" (S) · status: done · issue #40
**Serves:** beyond the brief (owner's request, 2026-10-20).
**Depends on:** T4.1, T3.1
**Acceptance criteria** (approved by the owner, 2026-10-20)
- [x] In the "Your details" dialog, Streamlit's in-field "Press Enter to apply" no longer appears in any input (weight, age, height in cm or ft/in) - manual browser check, metric and imperial, with a screenshot (2026-10-20: the instruction element stays in the DOM while a value is typed but is never shown; screenshot `docs/ui/t4.6-profile-dialog-hint.jpg`, imperial, "11" being typed in Height (in)).
- [x] The CSS rule targets only `[data-testid="InputInstructions"]` inside the dialog and is drawn only while the dialog is open; inputs elsewhere are unchanged - AppTest (`test_the_css_is_drawn_only_while_the_dialog_is_open`, `test_the_css_is_gone_after_the_dialog_is_dismissed`, `test_the_dialog_css_is_one_fixed_rule_scoped_to_the_dialog`). Wording adjusted with the owner's approval: a style-only `st.html` lives in Streamlit's event container, so it is "drawn only while the dialog is open" rather than "in the dialog".
- [x] The first line of the dialog is the hint "Type your values, then press Save. Values are checked when you save." - AppTest (`test_the_hint_is_the_first_line_of_the_dialog`).
- [x] A value typed and then saved by clicking Save directly (no Enter, no click elsewhere first) is saved - manual browser check (AppTest cannot simulate it): 77.3 kg in metric and 5 ft 11 in ("11" typed last) in imperial were saved.
- [x] The CSS is a fixed string with no script and no user or model content; `docs/UI_DESIGN.md` records the exception, its reason and its risk (a later Streamlit could rename the test id: the text would come back, nothing breaks); `docs/SECURITY.md` notes that `st.html` is used only with this fixed CSS - code review and tests (`test_the_dialog_css_is_one_fixed_rule_scoped_to_the_dialog`, `test_st_html_is_used_only_with_the_fixed_dialog_css`).
- [x] Existing profile tests pass; `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .` and `python -m pytest -q` pass (1,035 tests).
**Notes:** Streamlit 1.66 has no option to hide the text outside `st.form(enter_to_submit=False)`, and T4.1 chose not to use a form (the Metric/Imperial switch converts typed values), so the owner approved one scoped CSS rule as an exception to the "native first" principle.

### T4.8 Food lookup tab: a searchable dropdown of the USDA foods (S) · status: done · issue #85

- [x] The Food lookup tab has a searchable dropdown (`st.selectbox`, typing filters the list) with every food in the USDA table (sorted, one entry per food), with no food selected at the start - AppTest (`tests/test_app_targets.py`: `test_the_food_dropdown_lists_every_food_sorted_with_none_chosen`, `test_every_food_of_the_real_table_can_be_chosen_and_looked_up`); unit test on `food_names` (`tests/test_food_lookup.py`); checked in the browser (2026-10-22: typing "egg" lists the egg foods).

## Epic 5: Cost, export and guardrails

### T5.1 Token usage and cost (S) · status: done · issue #16
**Serves:** Medium 5 (extra, after the core); AC-10.
**Depends on:** T3.2
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `src/usage.py` sums tokens from every call of a turn (query translation, answer rounds, query embedding) and prices them from OpenRouter's models endpoint (cached a day; "price unknown" on failure) - unit tests with fake usage and prices (`tests/test_usage.py`: recording, prices read as exact decimals and unusable ones dropped, both lists read, no key sent, network failures, the day cache, a failed read not kept, cost with cached input and reasoning, turn and session sums, display); the calls of a turn (`tests/test_turn_usage.py`: every call in order, tokens kept after an error, a cut-off answer and a hidden reply counted, none for a question the guard stops; `tests/test_query_translation.py`: rewrite tokens with the fake and the real client).
- [x] The UI shows the turn's tokens and cost under the answer and the session totals in the sidebar - AppTest (`tests/test_app_usage.py`: `test_the_answer_shows_its_tokens_and_cost`, `test_without_prices_the_cost_says_price_unknown`, `test_the_cost_stays_with_each_answer_in_the_history`, `test_prices_are_read_once_per_turn_not_on_reruns`, `test_a_failed_price_read_is_tried_again_on_the_next_turn`, `test_a_rejected_question_shows_no_usage_and_reads_no_prices`, `test_a_failed_turn_shows_what_it_spent`, `test_the_sidebar_card_shows_the_answer_and_the_session_totals`, `test_the_session_total_says_when_some_prices_were_unknown`, `test_the_session_total_shows_before_any_turn_has_searched`, `test_the_answer_stays_in_the_history_whatever_the_price_read_does`).
- [x] (added, owner's approval 2026-10-21) The Details tab lists each call of the last answer (rewrite, query embedding, each answer round) with its tokens and cost, and the session total - AppTest (`test_details_lists_each_call_with_its_tokens_and_cost`).
- [x] (added, owner's approval 2026-10-21) The query embedding's tokens are counted exactly from the reply's `usage`, not estimated - tests with a fake server (`tests/test_llm.py`: `test_embeddings_report_the_tokens_the_api_counted`, `test_embedding_tokens_add_up_over_batches`, `test_embedding_tokens_not_reported_are_unknown`; `tests/test_turn_usage.py`: `test_the_query_embedding_records_the_tokens_the_api_reported`).
- [x] (added, owner's approval 2026-10-21) `scripts/live_check_llm.py` prints the app's cost of a plain and of a streamed chat call (the answer rounds' path) next to the cost and native tokens OpenRouter records (`/api/v1/generation`) - mocked tests (`tests/test_live_check_llm.py`) and the owner's O5 run on 2026-10-21 (`docs/TESTING.md`: the streamed call's tokens and cost equal OpenRouter's record; reasoning is not billed on top).

**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-20): usage in all three places (under the answer, the sidebar card, the Details tab); embedding tokens counted exactly; reasoning tokens coded to OpenAI's convention (included in the output tokens), to be confirmed by the O5 cost check. Defaults stated and not objected to: tokens spent before an error count; a failed price read is not cached (5 s timeout, "price unknown"); cached input priced at `input_cache_read`; USD, "<$0.0001" below a hundredth of a cent. T5.1 was paused for T3.3 (core first) and restarted on 2026-10-21. Built from `main` while T3.5 (#50, no PR yet) also changes `src/chat.py`: whichever merges second resolves the conflict.

### T5.2 Session summary PDF (M) · status: done · issue #17
**Serves:** Medium 7 and Easy 1 (extra); AC-9.
**Depends on:** T4.4
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `src/export_pdf.py` builds, in memory with reportlab, a PDF with the profile (in the chosen units), the targets, the questions and answers with their sources, the disclaimer and the credits - unit test reads the PDF text back (`tests/test_export_pdf.py`, read back with pypdf: `test_the_pdf_has_the_title_date_and_disclaimer`, `test_every_page_has_the_disclaimer_in_its_footer`, `test_the_profile_is_shown_in_the_chosen_units` (metric and imperial), `test_the_targets_come_from_the_profile_in_the_chosen_units` (g/kg and g/lb), `test_the_targets_notes_are_shown`, `test_each_question_and_answer_is_in_the_pdf_with_its_sources`, `test_a_turn_without_an_answer_shows_its_question_and_message` (every kind), `test_the_tool_results_follow_their_answer`, `test_the_credits_name_every_cited_article_in_full`, `test_the_credits_name_the_sources_behind_the_targets_and_the_formulas`, `test_the_credits_name_usda_only_when_a_food_was_looked_up`; `tests/test_app_export.py`: `test_the_pdf_built_on_click_holds_the_turn_that_just_ran`).
- [x] A download button offers it; no file is written on the server - AppTest and a unit test that checks no file is created (`tests/test_app_export.py`: `test_the_button_is_in_the_sidebar_and_off_in_an_empty_session`, `test_the_button_is_on_once_a_profile_is_added`, `test_the_button_is_on_after_a_question_without_a_profile`, `test_the_button_is_drawn_once_per_run_after_the_main_area`, `test_the_pdf_is_built_only_on_click_and_never_written`; `tests/test_export_pdf.py`: `test_building_the_pdf_writes_no_file` (no file opened for writing through `open`, `io.open` or `os.open`, the working folder stays empty); browser check with a fake model, `docs/TESTING.md`).
- [x] Non-Latin characters in source titles (e.g. "β") do not break the PDF - unit test (`test_non_latin_characters_in_source_titles_read_back_exactly`: #4's "β" and #16's U+2010 read back exactly; `test_the_fonts_cover_every_character_of_the_approved_sources`; `test_characters_the_font_lacks_do_not_break_the_pdf`: emoji, CJK and control characters). Needed a font: reportlab's have no Greek, so DejaVu Sans 2.37 is committed in `assets/fonts/` (owner's choice, 2026-10-20).
- [x] Answers are unescaped with `html.unescape`, so an entity the model copied from the escaped prompt (`&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&amp;`) shows as the character, as it does in the app's Markdown; then, since reportlab's `Paragraph` reads its text as markup (`<b>`, `<font>`, `<link href=...>`), they are escaped for it with `xml.sax.saxutils.escape` before any `Paragraph` (or drawn as plain text), so a `<` cannot break the PDF and model text cannot add a link or formatting - unit test: an answer holding "p &lt; 0.05" reads back "p < 0.05", and a raw `<b>x</b>` or `<link href="https://example.com">x</link>` reads back as literal text with no link annotation (follow-up from T3.4; owner's choices, 2026-10-20) (`test_an_entity_the_model_copied_reads_back_as_the_character`, `test_markup_in_an_answer_reads_back_as_literal_text_with_no_link` (`<b>`, `<link>`, `<a>`, `<font>`, an escaped `&lt;b&gt;` and a broken `<para>`), `test_markup_in_a_question_a_title_and_a_usda_name_stays_text`, `test_the_pdf_has_no_link_annotations_at_all`; a mutation check without the escape made 7 of them fail; after the code review the entities are decoded only in the inline text, after the Markdown cleanup, so "&gt; 5 g" stays "> 5 g" and is not read as a quote, and code keeps its entities as the app shows them: `test_an_entity_that_looks_like_markdown_reads_back_as_the_character` and the entity rows of `test_clean_answer_turns_markdown_into_plain_lines`).
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-20): DejaVu Sans for the fonts; the button in the sidebar; every turn in the PDF, as in the chat; answers as plain text (Markdown marks dropped, lists as bullets, links as text and URL). Defaults stated in the plan and kept: the targets computed from the profile with no model call (`registry.macro_targets()`), each answer's tool results under it, credits only for the sources the PDF uses, URLs as text with no link annotation, A4. In the review of PR #54 the owner chose to credit the font in every PDF and to print its licence (the Bitstream Vera and Arev parts of `assets/fonts/LICENSE`) word for word, as the licence asks for its notices with every copy (`test_the_credits_end_with_the_fonts_licence_word_for_word`). The app's own tool result tables still show g/kg in imperial; the PDF already follows the unit system (T4.7's criterion for the app).

### T5.3 Domain guardrails (S) · status: done · issue #18
**Serves:** core 3 (domain security); AC-12.
**Depends on:** T3.1, T4.2
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] Requests for steroid, SARM or other performance-enhancing drug dosing get a refusal; a set of benign supplement questions (creatine, caffeine) is not refused - parametrised tests (code-level check plus the owner's prompt rule) (`tests/test_guardrails.py`: `test_flagged_questions_get_their_guardrail_message` (the `PED_DOSING` strings), `test_benign_questions_are_not_refused_or_redirected` (creatine and caffeine doses, steroids without a dose request, plus the guard's benign list), `test_drug_dosing_wins_over_a_redirect`, `test_a_guardrail_rejection_makes_no_call`; `tests/test_chat.py`: `test_a_guardrail_question_opens_nothing_and_calls_no_model`, `test_the_answer_prompt_keeps_the_domain_guardrails`; `tests/test_app_chat.py`: `test_a_guardrail_question_shows_its_message_and_calls_no_model`, `test_a_creatine_or_caffeine_dose_question_is_answered`).
- [x] Mentions of being under 18, pregnancy, medical conditions or medication, and signs of disordered eating get a redirect message - parametrised tests (`tests/test_guardrails.py`: `test_flagged_questions_get_their_guardrail_message` (the `MINOR`, `PREGNANCY`, `MEDICAL` and `DISORDERED_EATING` strings, intakes below the calorie floor included), `test_benign_questions_are_not_refused_or_redirected` (near misses: "I'm 16 weeks into my cut", "I'm 17% body fat", "Does creatine harm the kidneys?", "I'm lactose intolerant", "a 500 kcal deficit per day"), `test_every_concern_has_a_message_and_a_flagged_example`, `test_the_low_intake_check_follows_the_configured_floor`, `test_a_guardrail_rejection_does_not_use_the_quota`; `tests/test_app_chat.py`: `test_a_guardrail_question_shows_its_message_and_calls_no_model`, one case per kind).
- [x] The disclaimer is visible on every page state - AppTest (`tests/test_app_chat.py`: `test_the_disclaimer_is_visible_in_every_chat_state`: new session, answer, no sources, rejected, guardrail, error; `tests/test_app_profile.py`: `test_disclaimer_stays_visible_in_every_profile_state`: dialog open, dialog with an error, profile saved, editing the profile; `tests/test_app_smoke.py`: `test_app_shows_disclaimer`).
**Notes:** owner's choices (2026-10-20): drug questions are refused only when they ask for a dose or use in the same sentence as the drug ("are SARMs safe?" reaches the model, which follows the prompt rule); redirects are fixed messages with no model call; medical conditions counted at first when said about the user or a group ("I have diabetes", "people with kidney disease"); after the review of PR #46 the owner chose that a disease named anywhere is redirected (research and prevention questions too), and only side effects (kidney, liver, heart or thyroid damage, problems or issues) and "GERD" (also a German first name) count when said about the user or a group, so "does creatine cause kidney damage?" is still answered from the research; "testosterone cycles" stays a drug term; medication counts on any mention; breastfeeding and the time after birth with pregnancy, lactose intolerance and allergies not at all; an intake per day below the calorie floor counts as a sign of disordered eating; a side effect counts after "I have", "my", "people with" or "safe/ok/bad for/with", not after a bare "with" (code review); a child under 10 counts only as the subject of the question ("for my 8-year-old"), so a parent asking about their own diet is answered (owner's approval, 2026-10-20). The messages were drafted by Claude and approved by the owner. German patterns are T7.1's.
