### T7.4 German input guard and guardrails (M) · status: done · issue #63
**Serves:** Hard 4 (multi-language support), as an extra after the core; core 3 (security measures relevant to the domain) and Medium 3 (prompt injection protection) for German input; AC-16. Beyond the PRD's original English-only scope (owner's request, 2026-10-20).
**Depends on:** T1.3, T5.3
**Decision (owner, 2026-10-21):** T7.1 turned out closer to L than M at its start, so its German safety checks were split into this ticket and come first, in their own PR; the German UI (T7.1) ships only once they are merged. The two criteria below moved here from T7.1; the other three were added with the split and approved by the owner.
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] The input guard rejects German prompt-injection strings (with ß, ss, ü and ue spellings) and passes German benign nutrition questions - parametrised tests with our own strings (`tests/test_guard_de.py`: `test_german_injection_attempts_are_rejected`, 69 attack strings; `test_german_benign_questions_pass`, 49 questions; `test_german_spells_each_umlaut_three_ways`).
- [x] The domain guardrails' code-level checks catch German requests (performance-enhancing drug dosing, under 18, pregnancy, medical conditions, disordered eating, an intake below the calorie floor) - parametrised tests with German attack and benign strings (`tests/test_guardrails_de.py`: `test_german_flagged_questions_get_their_guardrail_message`, 135 strings over the five concerns; `test_german_benign_questions_are_not_refused_or_redirected`, 110 questions plus the guard's 49; `test_every_concern_has_a_german_example`; `test_the_german_low_intake_check_follows_the_configured_floor`).
- [x] The English checks behave as before - the existing guard and guardrail tests pass unchanged (`tests/test_guard.py`, `tests/test_guardrails.py`, `tests/test_prompt_injection.py`), and English questions with a word the German lists share still pass (`ENGLISH_UNCHANGED` in `tests/test_guardrails_de.py`, 14 questions: the Krebs cycle in five spellings, the chemist Hans Krebs, "tumor", "Hashimoto", "depressive", "diclofenac", "steroid hormone", "diagnose").
- [x] The German patterns stay fast on long input - timing tests like the English one (`test_the_german_patterns_stay_fast_on_long_input`, `test_the_german_guardrails_stay_fast_on_long_input`).
- [x] `docs/SECURITY.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` say the patterns cover English and German, with the German known limits - review.
**Notes:**
- The patterns run on casefolded NFKC text, once with accents and once without (`detection_views()`), so ß is always ss and ü is seen as both ü and u. `german()` in `src/german.py` lets each umlaut in a pattern also match the plain vowel and the typed ue/oe/ae; the patterns are written with ss. No umlaut goes inside a lookbehind (`german()` would make it variable-width).
- The messages stay English until T7.1 translates the UI. The German test strings are our own writing.

### T7.1 Language switch: English and German UI (M) · status: done · issue #33
**Serves:** Hard 4 (multi-language support), as an extra after the core; AC-16. Changes the PRD's English-only decision: beyond the PRD (owner's request, 2026-10-20).
**Depends on:** T4.4, T4.7, T5.3 (so most UI text and the guardrails exist before they are translated), T1.3, T7.4 (the German guard and guardrails, split from this ticket on 2026-10-21)
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `src/i18n.py` holds every user-facing UI string in English and German behind one lookup function; a test fails if a key exists in one language but not the other - unit test (`tests/test_i18n.py`: `test_every_key_exists_in_both_languages`, `test_each_text_is_filled_and_has_the_same_placeholders_in_both_languages` for each of the keys; the English entries that the model, the guard or the PDF also use are checked against their modules).
- [x] The header has a language control (a `:material/translate:` icon next to an EN | DE segmented control labelled "Language", label collapsed, `required=True` so it can never be deselected to `None`); switching it changes all UI text (sidebar, tabs, buttons, errors, guard and guardrail messages, disclaimer) - AppTest in both languages (`tests/test_app_language.py`: the control's label, collapsed label, options and `required`; the German header, disclaimer, tabs, sidebar and PDF button; the profile form and its errors; the targets with German numbers; a guard rejection, a guardrail redirect, the "not covered" message and a past message switched back; the Details tab, its usage table and the tool results after an answer, with a check that no English UI text is left on the page; the Food lookup tab's four German messages; every turn's message key checked to exist in both languages (`turn_codes_are_keys` in `tests/conftest.py`); the existing English AppTests pass (one source scan now looks for `_t("theme.hint")`); accessible name checked in the browser, `docs/TESTING.md`).
- [x] The default language follows the browser locale (`st.context.locale`: German for `de*`, English otherwise, including `None`; changed by T9.5 at the owner's choice of 2026-11-02: English for every session) and the user's choice is kept for the session - unit test on the locale mapping and AppTest (`test_a_german_browser_locale_gives_german`, `test_any_other_locale_or_none_gives_english`; `test_a_german_browser_starts_in_german`, `test_the_choice_is_kept_for_the_session`, `test_the_browser_locale_is_read_only_at_the_start`; the locale is set on a session's first run, checked in the browser).
- [x] Source titles, licence names and USDA food names stay in English; the Food lookup tab tells German users to enter English food names - AppTest (`test_an_answer_keeps_its_text_and_its_sources_stay_english`, `test_the_food_lookup_tells_german_users_to_use_english_names`).
- [x] The German texts, the medical disclaimer in particular, are reviewed by the owner or a German speaker before merge - manual check recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md` (the owner reviewed all 259 texts on 2026-10-21, the four Food lookup tab texts after the others, no change asked).
The German guard and guardrail criteria moved to T7.4 (owner's decision, 2026-10-21).
**Notes:**
- The German safety checks are in T7.4 (split from this ticket), not T7.2, because a German UI invites German input even if T7.2 is cut; T7.4 merges first.
- Owner's choices at the start (2026-10-21): the summary PDF stays English here and becomes German in T7.2 (its criterion); tool-produced text (the targets' message and notes, the TDEE method, food and meal plan messages) stays English in the chat's tool results and the My targets tab, while the Food lookup tab, which calls no model, shows its own messages in German (changed in the review of PR #74); the owner reviews the German texts. Past turns' fixed messages (guard, errors, "not covered") follow the language chosen now; the model's answers stay in the language they were written in (default stated in the Q phase).
- Many messages come from `src/` (guard rejections and rate limit in `src/guard.py`, LLM errors in `src/llm.py`, `IndexNotReadyError` in `src/rag/store.py`, profile validation in `src/profile.py`). `src/` returns message keys or codes and the UI translates them. Tool notes stay English, so the tool result the UI shows matches what the model received. The size was rechecked at the start (closer to L), hence the T7.4 split. `chat.format_profile()` reads the profile labels for the model's `<profile>` block, so translated labels must stay out of it.
- The German UI texts are our own writing. Every UI string added after T7.1 needs both languages.

### T7.2 German answers end to end (M) · status: done · issue #34
**Serves:** Hard 4 (multi-language support), as an extra after the core; AC-16. Beyond the PRD (owner's request, 2026-10-20).
**Depends on:** T7.1, T3.2, O1 (a language line in the system prompt), O2 (German evaluation questions), O5 (German live run)
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] The chosen language reaches the answer step as a fixed value (`en` or `de`), never taken from user text; the answer uses that language and the same `[S#]` citations - integration test with a scripted fake model on the built messages (`tests/test_chat.py`: `test_the_chosen_language_s_part_ends_the_system_prompt`, `test_the_user_message_is_the_same_in_both_languages`, `test_the_question_cannot_choose_the_answer_language`, `test_an_unknown_language_is_refused_before_any_call`, `test_a_missing_or_empty_language_part_stops_the_turn_before_any_call`, `test_each_language_reads_only_its_own_part`, `[S1 und S2]` in `test_cited_labels_reads_the_labels_in_brackets`; `tests/test_app_language.py`: `test_the_answer_model_gets_the_language_chosen_in_the_header`, `test_an_english_question_in_the_german_ui_gets_the_german_part`). Live (O5): all five German answers in German with `[S#]` cards.
- [x] Query translation turns a German question into English rewrites, so vector and BM25 retrieval run in English - unit test with a fake model (`test_a_german_question_is_searched_with_its_english_rewrites`, `test_the_english_rewrites_find_what_the_german_words_do_not`, `test_the_query_translation_prompt_asks_for_english_rewrites`; T3.8's gate for German pinned by `test_a_german_question_with_no_close_chunk_of_its_own_is_not_covered`). Live (O5): every German question got three English rewrites.
- [x] The food tool's description tells the model to pass English food names, so a German food question still finds the USDA food - test on the tool description, plus one German food question in O2 (`tests/test_tools_registry.py`: `test_the_food_tool_tells_the_model_to_pass_english_names`, `test_the_meal_plan_s_foods_follow_the_same_english_rule`; live: de4 "Haferflocken" matched "Oats, whole grain, rolled, old fashioned", 568 kcal and 20,2 g protein).
- [x] Guardrail refusals and redirects are answered in the chosen language - parametrised tests (`test_guardrail_refusals_and_redirects_follow_the_chosen_language`: one German question per concern and a low intake, in the German and the English UI, 12 cases, no model call; `test_a_german_pdf_shows_fixed_messages_in_german` for the PDF). Refusals the model writes itself (out of scope, weight cuts) follow the language part (criterion 1).
- [x] Source passages are never presented as translated quotes; summaries keep the meaning (`.claude/rules/copyright.md` rule 7) - prompt rule plus a test on the built system prompt (the owner's rules in `answer_system.md` and both language parts, O1; `test_the_built_prompt_never_presents_a_translation_as_a_quote`, `test_the_german_part_names_the_german_ui_labels`). Live (O5): no translated quote; de5's one quote is the source's exact English.
- [x] If T5.2 is built, the PDF uses the chosen language - unit test reads back German headings (`tests/test_export_pdf.py`: `test_the_german_pdf_reads_back_its_german_headings` and nine more German PDF tests; `tests/test_app_export.py`: `test_the_german_ui_downloads_a_german_pdf`, `test_the_pdf_keeps_the_language_of_the_run_that_drew_the_button`). The owner reviewed the new German texts (2026-10-21).
- [x] The owner's German evaluation questions are run against the real app and the results recorded in `docs/TESTING.md` - owner live check (O5, 2026-10-21, `scripts/run_evaluation.py --language de` and `scripts/live_check_retrieval.py`; "Evaluation results (T7.2, German)": 3 yes, 2 partly, none wrong; judgement approved by the owner).
**Notes:** the knowledge base stays English. Since T3.8 (2026-10-21) the English rewrites widen the search but do not decide whether the sources cover a question: only the question's own vector does, against `RETRIEVAL_MAX_DISTANCE`, which was calibrated on English questions. A German question whose own wording is further than the limit from the English chunks gets `NO_SOURCES_MESSAGE` even when its English rewrites come close, so the second criterion alone does not make German retrieval work. T3.8's live check records one or two German questions; how T7.2 handles this (e.g. a limit measured on German questions, or letting the English rewrites decide for a German question) is the owner's choice. Owner's choice (2026-10-21): measure first; the five German questions' own nearest chunks were at 0.319-0.617, all within 0.62, so the gate stays as it is. Other choices at the start: the language parts are fixed text the code joins to the end of the answer prompt (not a block in the user message); the PDF writes every app text in the language (tools' messages, notes, credit lines and the font licence stay English); the German language part was drafted in German by the owner and translated into English (the repo is English).

### T7.3 Light and dark mode (S) · status: done · issue #35
**Serves:** beyond the brief (owner's request, 2026-10-20); supports core 5 (intuitive UI); AC-17.
**Depends on:** T3.1 (writes both themes in `.streamlit/config.toml`)
**Decision (owner, 2026-10-20):** use Streamlit's built-in appearance switch (System / Light / Dark at the top of the menu; Streamlit 1.66 has no separate Settings entry, found in T7.3). Streamlit 1.66 cannot change the theme from the script, and a custom CSS toggle would break the "no custom CSS" rule and be fragile.
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `.streamlit/config.toml` defines both `[theme.light]` and `[theme.dark]` with the colours in `docs/UI_DESIGN.md`, so the menu offers Light, Dark and System - test that parses the config (`tests/test_theme.py`: `test_the_config_defines_both_modes`, `test_the_themes_match_the_ui_design_doc`, `test_each_mode_sets_the_core_colours_as_hex`).
- [x] The toolbar setting (`client.toolbarMode`) keeps the theme switch (the plan said "the Settings entry"; Streamlit 1.66 has none, reworded with the owner's approval, 2026-10-20) available to users - test on the config value plus a manual check (`test_the_toolbar_keeps_the_theme_switch`; browser check in "auto" and "viewer" mode, `docs/TESTING.md`).
- [x] A short hint in the sidebar tells users where to switch light or dark mode (in both languages once T7.1 is merged) - AppTest (`tests/test_app_profile.py`: the hint without a profile, right under the privacy note with one, and its wording). The German text comes with T7.1.
- [x] The badges and source cards are readable in both modes - manual check in both modes, recorded in `docs/TESTING.md` (2026-10-20), plus contrast tests in `tests/test_theme.py`. The macro bar, which T4.4 adds, moved to a T4.4 criterion (owner's choice, 2026-10-20).
- [x] No `unsafe_allow_html` is added, and `st.html` is used only with T4.6's fixed CSS constant (`config.PROFILE_DIALOG_CSS`, allowlisted by name) - a source-scan test (T4.6's `st.html` test, T3.4's, or added here if neither covers it): both existing scans cover it (`test_st_html_is_used_only_with_the_fixed_dialog_css` and T3.4's `test_nothing_in_the_app_renders_with_unsafe_allow_html` and `test_the_app_uses_no_raw_html_components` in `tests/test_prompt_injection.py`), so none was added.
**Notes:** T3.1 already writes both theme sections; this ticket adds the config test, the toolbar check, the hint and the two-mode check.

## Epic 8: Sprint 2 review follow-ups

Added by the owner on 2026-10-25 from the reviewer's feedback on the Sprint 2 project (summarised in `docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`, "The Sprint 2 review"). Order agreed with the owner: T8.1, then T1.8 and T8.2, then T8.3 and T8.4, before Epic 9; T8.5 and T8.6 after the agent works. On 2026-10-25 the owner put T9.1 (docs only) before T1.8; the rest of the order stands. Review point 3 (tool questions get unrelated passages) is not a ticket here: T9.3's agentic RAG answers it.

**Serves:** The Sprint 2 review, point 2 (`app.py` of about 1,380 lines against the project's layering; `docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md` names T8.1 for it); the Sprint 3 evaluation, core concepts: good code organisation (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`), as in the Sprint 2 evaluation (`docs/BRIEF.md`); Sprint 3 core 5 (documentation: the technical decisions made, here the UI layout in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/UI_DESIGN.md`); prepares Sprint 3 core 3 (a user interface for every function) for the UI Epic 9 adds (sign-in, Medium 4; memory, Medium 2; approvals).
**Depends on:** -
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] A `views/` package holds the UI by concern (at least `chat.py`, `targets.py`, `food.py`, `details.py`, `charts.py`, `profile_form.py`, `sources.py`, `export.py`; the exact split is set in the Structure phase); the functions that build chart specs and table rows do not import `streamlit` and are unit-tested without AppTest - their unit tests, plus a test that their modules do not import `streamlit`. Done: 16 modules (`views/` in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3); `views/charts.py` and `views/tables.py` take the language as a parameter; `tests/test_views_charts.py` (10 tests), `tests/test_views_tables.py` (48 tests), and `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_the_spec_and_row_builders_do_not_import_streamlit` (an `ast` scan of their imports).
- [x] `app.py` has at most 150 lines and only composes the page: page config, session defaults, header, sidebar and tab routing; it defines no chart, table or card builder - `tests/test_app_layout.py` (line count, and the module-level functions it defines are on a short allowlist). Done: `app.py` has 71 lines and one function, `_main_area()` (the allowlist); `test_app_py_has_at_most_150_lines`, `test_app_py_defines_only_the_allowed_functions`.
- [x] No behaviour changes: no test is deleted, skipped or loosened (only import paths change), the number of tests is not lower, and coverage of `views/` plus `app.py` is not lower than `app.py`'s before - the before and after counts and coverage in the PR description. Done: 2,985 tests before (`app.py`: 646 statements, 10 missed, 98%), 3,052 after (`app.py` and `views/`: 736 statements, the same 10 missed, 99%); the only changed lines in existing tests are four tests that read the source of `app.py` (the theme hint's place in the sidebar, the single "Last answer" slot, the PDF function's body, the `st.fragment` decorator) and the file lists of three scan tests, pointed at the new modules with the same assertions.

Done: `test_nothing_in_the_app_renders_with_unsafe_allow_html` and `test_the_app_uses_no_raw_html_components` (`APP_FILES`), `test_st_html_is_used_only_with_the_fixed_dialog_css`, `test_the_app_opens_no_image_file` now list `app.py`, `views/` and `src/`; `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_the_unsafe_html_and_components_scans_list_the_views` and `test_the_html_and_image_scans_glob_the_views_folder` fail if one of the three stops listing `views/`.
- [x] In the browser the four tabs, the profile dialog, the About dialog, the units switch, EN | DE, both themes and the PDF download look and work as before; one question with sources is answered - manual check with screenshots, the answered question by the owner (needs the key). Done by Claude on 2026-10-25 with fake models (`docs/TESTING.md`, live checks; screenshots `docs/ui/t8.1-*.jpg`): all of it except clicking the PDF button (a download needs the owner's permission; the button was enabled and its content is covered by `tests/test_app_export.py`). The owner's answered question with the real models (O13), on 2026-10-25: q1 answered with [S1]-[S5] from three sources, a tool result asking for the profile, tokens and cost, and the Details tab (`docs/TESTING.md`; screenshots `docs/ui/t8.1-owner-real-*.png`).
- [x] `CLAUDE.md` ("Where things are", code conventions: UI in `views/`, logic in `src/`), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 3 and `docs/UI_DESIGN.md` name the new layout - review. Done, and the same layout is named in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 7, `docs/SECURITY.md` (where the scans and the controls live), `docs/TESTING.md` and the two reviewer agents' instructions (`.claude/agents/`).

### T8.2 Structured turn logging with no personal data (M) · status: done · issue #91
**Serves:** The Sprint 2 review, point 1 (no logging or request tracing; `docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md` names T8.2 for it); Sprint 3 core 4 (proper error handling, real-world use) and the evaluation, technical (appropriate security: no personal data in the logs). Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): core 4 (proper error handling: errors leave a trace), Medium 10 (logging and monitoring) and the evaluation, technical (appropriate security). PRD AC-33 (in part, until T9.6 adds stored values).
**Depends on:** T1.8 (its catch-all writes the first log line; this ticket builds the logger it uses), T8.1 (order agreed with the owner)
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `src/observability.py` has `configure_logging()`, which puts a JSON formatter on the app's logger and writes to stderr only (no file handler); calling it twice leaves one handler; the level comes from `config.LOG_LEVEL` - unit tests (each line parses as JSON with `ts`, `level`, `logger`, `event`; handler count; no `FileHandler`). Done: the app's logger is `src` (every module's `logging.getLogger(__name__)` reports through it, `propagate` off); `tests/test_observability.py` (`test_each_line_is_json_with_the_four_base_fields`, `test_configuring_twice_leaves_one_handler`, `test_the_handler_writes_to_stderr_and_no_handler_is_a_file`, `test_the_level_comes_from_the_config`); `tests/conftest.py` removes the handler after each test.
- [x] Each chat turn gets a `turn_id` (UUID4), carried in `TurnResult`, and writes exactly one INFO record `turn` on every ending (answer, no sources, rejected, guardrail, error) with `turn_id`, `kind`, `code`, `language`, `chunks`, `source_ids`, `tools`, `tool_statuses`, `tokens`, `cost_usd` (as text), `duration_ms` and `profile_sent` - `tests/test_chat.py` with `caplog`, one test per ending. Done (owner's choices, 2026-10-26): `cost_usd` uses only the prices already read (`usage.PriceCache.peek()`; `null` when unknown, so the first turn of a process has `null`), as reading them is a network call the view makes after the answer is shown; `source_ids` are the sources of the chunks the answer model got, next to `chunks`. `test_every_ending_writes_exactly_one_turn_record` (five endings), `test_the_turn_id_is_a_new_uuid4_for_each_turn`, `test_the_answer_record_counts_chunks_sources_and_tokens`, `test_the_record_names_the_tools_and_their_statuses`, the cost tests, `tests/test_usage.py` (`peek`).
- [x] An `LLMError` writes a WARNING with its `diagnostic`, status code and code; a guard rejection writes a WARNING with the `Rejection` reason and code; a tool result with an error or invalid status writes a WARNING with the tool name and status; each carries the `turn_id` - unit tests. Done: `llm_error`, `guard_rejected` (rejections, rate limits and domain guardrails) and `tool_failed` in `src/chat.py`; `tests/test_chat.py` (`test_a_model_error_writes_a_warning_with_its_diagnostic`, `test_a_guard_rejection_writes_a_warning_with_its_reason`, `test_a_rate_limit_writes_a_guard_warning`, `test_a_failed_tool_writes_a_warning_with_its_name_and_status`, `test_a_tool_the_model_made_up_is_logged_without_its_name`, `test_an_accepted_question_writes_no_warning`); a tool that fails in a turn that then ends in an error is logged too (`test_a_tool_failure_before_a_model_error_is_still_logged`, `test_a_tool_loop_error_logs_every_round_s_tool_runs`). T1.8's line is now the ERROR record `turn_error` with a `diagnostic` field.
- [x] The formatter writes only allowlisted fields: an unknown `extra` key is dropped - unit test. Done: `test_a_field_that_is_not_allowed_is_dropped`, `test_the_message_arguments_and_exceptions_are_not_written`, `test_every_allowed_field_is_written`.
- [x] No record holds the question, the rewrites, the answer, chunk text, profile values or the key: every ending is run with a profile of distinctive values (87.3 kg, 181.7 cm, age 37), a question containing `CANARY-Q` and a fake key `sk-or-CANARY`, and none of them appears in any formatted record - `tests/test_observability.py`. Done: `test_no_record_holds_the_question_the_answer_the_profile_or_the_key` runs eleven endings (answer, answer with tools, no sources, rejected, guardrail, model error, tool with hostile input, real SDK error, index not ready, missing key, unexpected error); `test_the_canary_run_sent_the_profile_to_the_model` and `test_the_canary_check_catches_a_leak` show the check can fail.
- [x] The app and the scripts call `configure_logging()` once at start - AppTest and script tests. Done (all eight scripts, owner's choice): `test_the_app_configures_logging_once_however_often_it_reruns`, `test_every_script_configures_logging_first_in_main` (an `ast` check of each `scripts/*.py`), `test_a_script_calls_configure_logging`.
- [x] `docs/SECURITY.md` (what is logged, what never is), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 10 and `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md` (the never-log list) are updated - review. Done: a row in SECURITY's threat table, the "Logging" paragraph and the folder layout in ARCHITECTURE, a "Logs" section in the rule file.
- [x] `ruff check .`, `ruff format --check .` and `python -m pytest -q` pass - CI. Done: lint, test and security green on PR #121.
**Notes:** source IDs and tool names are not personal data; tool arguments can be (a weight, a food name typed by the user), so they are never logged. T9.8 reuses `turn_id` to tie a log line to a trace.

### T8.3 At most three chunks per source in the fused list (S) · status: done · issue #92
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): the Sprint 2 review, point 4 (one article can fill all six retrieved places; the brief names T8.3 for it). Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): Hard 1 (hybrid search) and core 1 (RAG: standard retrieval, similarity search), which it improves.
**Depends on:** T8.1 (order agreed with the owner), O13
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `config.MAX_CHUNKS_PER_SOURCE = 3`; the fusion ranks every candidate first, then keeps at most that many chunks per source, then cuts to `RETRIEVAL_TOP_K`; when too few other sources are left to fill the places, the skipped chunks fill them in fused order (the owner may change this in the Q phase) - unit tests in `tests/test_hybrid.py` (six chunks of A and two of B give 3 A, 2 B, then 1 A; a single-source list still gives six; ties keep their order). Done: `MAX_CHUNKS_PER_SOURCE` in `src/config.py`; `rrf(..., max_per_source=None)` in `src/rag/hybrid.py`; `tests/test_hybrid.py::test_rrf_keeps_at_most_three_chunks_of_a_source_then_fills_with_the_skipped_ones`, `test_rrf_with_enough_other_sources_leaves_the_skipped_chunks_out`, `test_rrf_with_one_source_still_gives_six_chunks`, `test_rrf_cap_keeps_the_order_of_equal_scores`, `test_rrf_cap_can_be_set_per_call`. The skipped chunks come after the kept ones (the ticket's "3 A, 2 B, then 1 A"); owner approved on 2026-10-26.
- [x] The `[S#]` order is the fused order and each chunk keeps its smallest distance; the coverage gate (the question's own vector list against `RETRIEVAL_MAX_DISTANCE`) is unchanged; the HMB test (AC-4) passes - existing and new tests. Done: `test_rrf_cap_keeps_the_smallest_distance_of_each_chunk`, `test_hybrid_search_lets_a_second_source_in_before_a_fourth_chunk_of_the_first`; the gate in `hybrid_search` is untouched, and the existing `test_hybrid.py`, `test_chat.py` (the HMB test) and `test_compare_retrieval.py` tests pass unchanged.
- [x] `scripts/compare_retrieval.py` uses the same capped list, and its hit rates are not lower than T3.6's recorded run - owner live check (O13), recorded in `docs/TESTING.md`. Code done: the script's hybrid row calls `hybrid_search`, so it is capped (`tests/test_compare_retrieval.py::test_the_hybrid_row_is_capped_per_source_but_the_vector_row_is_not`); owner's run on 2026-10-26: hybrid 10 of 10, as T3.6 (`docs/TESTING.md`, "Per-source cap results (T8.3)").
- [x] `scripts/run_evaluation.py --repeat 3` records whether q5 now finds #12 and q10 finds #8, and that no expected source is lost - owner live check (O13), recorded in `docs/TESTING.md` next to T6.1's results. Done by the owner on 2026-10-26 (51 runs, $0.0854): recorded in `docs/TESTING.md`: q5 does **not** find #12 and q10 does **not** find #8 (0 of 3 runs each, as in T6.1); none lost by the cap; q1's #15 (1 of 2 runs in T6.1, 0 of 3 now) dropped for another reason, see `docs/TESTING.md`. The criterion is to record the outcome, so it is met; the goal behind it is not reached for these two questions, and widening the candidate lists was the open decision, settled on 2026-10-26 by the depth measurement (`docs/TESTING.md`, "Candidate depth (T8.3 follow-up)"): the lists stay as they are.
**Notes:** the candidate lists are as long as before (`RETRIEVAL_TOP_K` each), so a second source that no list ranks in its top six cannot get in; owner's decision (2026-10-26): they stay `RETRIEVAL_TOP_K` long for now. The live check passed the hit-rate check (hybrid 10 of 10) but q5 and q10 still miss their second source (#12, #8), so longer lists or MMR were to be decided before T8.4 takes its baseline; the depth measurement of 2026-10-26 settled it: q10's #8 is beyond rank 30 in both lists and q5's #12 is already in the top 6 when the question is searched alone, so neither longer lists nor MMR is built. MMR was the reviewer's other option; it needs every candidate's vector and a tuning value, which is why the cap came first.

### T8.4 Evaluation results as JSON and a committed baseline (M) · status: done · issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#2 (the copy of #93)

Done (owner's choice, 2026-10-26: per expected source, not per question): `eval_results.summarise()`, in the file's `summary`; `tests/test_eval_results.py` (`test_the_summary_counts_expected_sources_found_and_cited_per_run_and_averaged`, the unscored questions, error endings before and after the search, null rates).

Done: the owner's run of 2026-10-26 12:40 (`main` at bd0304c with this ticket's uncommitted code, so the file says `dirty`; 51 runs, no error, $0.0859; found rate 0.8235, cited rate 0.8039) is `data/eval_baseline.json`, recorded in `docs/TESTING.md` ("Baseline (T8.4)" and the Live checks table); it is committed with this ticket's PR. T8.3's open decision is settled: the depth measurement of 2026-10-26 (`docs/TESTING.md`, "Candidate depth (T8.3 follow-up)") found q10's #8 beyond rank 30 in both lists and q5's #12 already in the top 6, so the lists stay `RETRIEVAL_TOP_K` long and the baseline is taken on this retrieval.
- [x] `scripts/check_eval_baseline.py <results.json>` exits 1 when the found rate or the cited rate is below its baseline minus `config.EVAL_TOLERANCE` (set from the spread of the three baseline runs) and 0 otherwise, printing each rate and its baseline - unit tests with fixture files. Done: the script and `eval_results.compare()`; `tests/test_check_eval_baseline.py` (equal, above, at the limit, just below, which rate fell, the tolerance from the config, the default baseline path, commit lines, a note for other models, exit 2 for another language, schema, set of scored questions or expected sources, for a scored run that ended in an error (in the results or the baseline, each named with its own fix; owner's decision, 2026-10-26), for a malformed run entry, for null rates and rates outside 0 to 1, for broken and missing files, and a file written by the run read back). Beyond the ticket: exit code 2 when the run cannot be compared, as a new question changes the baseline (the Notes). `config.EVAL_TOLERANCE` is 0.06, set from the spread of the baseline run's three askings (the largest distance of one asking from the average, 0.0588, one expected source of 17, rounded up; `tests/test_check_eval_baseline.py::test_the_committed_baseline_reads_back_and_its_own_runs_stay_within_the_tolerance`).

### T8.5 An offline retrieval regression suite in CI (M) · status: done · issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#3 (the copy of #94)
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): the Sprint 2 review, point 5 (evaluation is manual and not part of the development loop: catch recall regressions with no key, in ordinary CI). Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): Hard 7 (RAG evaluation), which it runs on every PR, and Hard 1 (hybrid search), which it guards.
**Depends on:** T8.4
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] A fixture index is built in a temporary directory from short texts we wrote ourselves (no article text: copyright policy rule 8; owner's choice, 2026-10-25), with a deterministic fake embedder, through the same ingest metadata and `store` code as the real index - fixture in `tests/fixtures/`. Done: `tests/fixtures/retrieval/sources.json` (twelve made-up sources, read by `load_manifest()`, the loader of `data/sources.json`) and `suite.json` (their 30 short sections, one chunk each, all written for the tests); `ingest.chunk_article()` and `store.build_index()` build the index in pytest's temporary folder, with `ConceptEmbeddings` (each of 14 concept word groups is a dimension, plus a small constant; `DeterministicFakeEmbedding`'s random vectors are about 1.0 apart, so no question would pass the gate); `test_the_fixture_index_is_built_by_the_ingest_and_store_code`, `test_the_concept_embeddings_put_shared_concepts_close_and_off_topic_text_far`.
- [x] A suite of fixture questions with expected chunk IDs runs the real pipeline (filters, the gate, BM25, RRF, the per-source cap) and fails if the hit rate at `RETRIEVAL_TOP_K` drops below the rate recorded in the fixture - `tests/test_retrieval_regression.py`, run by the ordinary CI test job with no key. Done: nine questions through `chat.find_chunks()` (the search the agent's tool runs, the filter retry included) with the fixture's plan in place of query translation and the config's own `RETRIEVAL_TOP_K`, gate, `RRF_K` and cap. Owner's choices (2026-11-09): the hit rate counts expected chunks, not questions (recorded 0.8888, 8 of 9); off-topic questions have their own no-sources rate (recorded 1.0, 2 of 2); one known miss stands for q10's #8 (T8.3). The training retry is checked to return training chunks only (`only_topic`, code review round 1). `test_the_rates_do_not_drop_below_the_recorded_ones`, `test_only_the_known_misses_are_missed`, `test_the_fixture_questions_and_recorded_rates_are_well_formed`.
- [x] A deliberate break (for example the cap set to 1, or BM25 removed) makes the suite fail - shown once in the PR description. Done: `MAX_CHUNKS_PER_SOURCE = 1` in `src/config.py`, run once and reverted (the rate test and the known-misses test fail, `cap_second_chunk` losing `2-1`), in the PR description; and, by the owner's choice (2026-11-09), six permanent tests (`test_a_deliberate_break_drops_a_rate_below_the_recorded_one`: a cap of one, no cap, no BM25, the plan's filters ignored, no gate; and `test_a_retry_that_searches_everything_lets_a_nutrition_chunk_into_the_training_answer`), so the fixture stays sensitive to each step.
**Notes:** this catches pipeline bugs, not embedding quality; T8.6 covers the real model. The fake vectors are meaningful only within the fixture: a legitimate change to `RETRIEVAL_TOP_K`, the gate or the cap can move a recorded rate, and lowering one is a decision recorded in `docs/TESTING.md`. Not covered: query translation (each question's plan is fixed in the fixture), the chunk splitting of long sections (each section is one chunk), and the nutrition-only retry (`tests/test_chat.py` covers it).

Done: the owner's run of 2026-11-09 on `main` at 1cf566b (run 42602414526, nutrition, 3 askings) passed every step: 31 sources downloaded, 2302 chunks indexed, 51 runs with no error, only the `.json` uploaded, the check exit 0 (found 0.7843, cited 0.7647, within T8.4's baseline); `docs/TESTING.md`, "Live evaluation workflow, first run (T8.6)".

Workflow test: `test_the_only_secret_is_the_evaluation_key`, `test_the_key_reaches_only_the_two_steps_that_call_openrouter`, `test_no_script_names_the_key_or_an_expression`, `test_the_checkout_keeps_no_token`. The first run's log reviewed by Claude (2026-11-09): the key only as `***` in the two steps' `env`, no `sk-or` string, no question, answer or chunk text (`docs/TESTING.md`). **Not met: a separate key with a credit limit.** The first run used the school's course key, very likely the app's own, as the owner has no other OpenRouter key; its credit limit is the school's. Owner's decision (2026-11-09): the school's key must not be exposed in any way, so the secret was deleted right after the run and stays unset until the owner has a separate key of their own (O11). ## Epic 9: Sprint 3 agent

Added by the owner on 2026-10-25 for the Sprint 3 project, which builds on this app (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`); deadline **2026-11-23**. Rewritten the same day by T9.1 for the **coach-first scope** the owner chose (`docs/PRD.md`, "Sprint 3: the agent (coach-first workspace)"; decisions D9-D21 in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`; the planned design in its section 16): a coach invites gymers, follows their nutrition and training, and approves the weekly reviews the agent drafts. Earlier owner decisions that stand: a hand-built LangGraph `StateGraph`; conversations never written to disk ("level 2"); real sign-in with `st.login` (Google, OIDC) before any body data is stored, with a guest mode with nothing stored; observability as local JSON logs (T8.2) plus LangSmith with personal data masked. Chat summaries in long-term memory moved to the Capstone (T9.1). Reworked on 2026-11-02 by T9.24 for the coach-first product model the owner chose that day: the AI serves the coach, and a gymer has no AI chat; the gymer logs, shares and reads what the coach approved (`docs/PRD.md`, "Sprint 3").

**This changes the privacy rule:** today the profile lives in the session only and is never written to disk (`.claude/rules/security-privacy.md`, PRD feature 4). T9.6, which adds the store, changes this for signed-in users and updates the rule, the PRD and `docs/SECURITY.md` in the same PR.

**For maximum points (P0):** Medium 2 (memory: T9.4's short-term memory, done; T9.7's long-term memory for the coach), Medium 4 (authentication and personalisation: T9.5, T9.6), Hard 1 (agentic RAG: T9.3, done); Medium 1 and Medium 8 are kept from Sprint 2. **Still met after T9.24** (checked against `docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): Sprint 3 still reaches "at least 2 medium and 1 hard" from new work alone, as none of the three rests on a gymer's chat. Medium 2 asks for long- or short-term memory in LangChain or LangGraph: the guest's and the coach's chats keep T9.4's short-term memory, and T9.7 moves long-term memory, the course's "remember" tool with a person's approval, to the coach. Medium 4 asks for user authentication and personalisation: T9.5 signs coaches and gymers in with their own role and pages, T9.6 keeps each user's data, and T9.7's preferences personalise the coach's chat. Hard 1 is agentic RAG: the search tool of the guest's and the coach's chats (T9.3). **Should:** T9.17 (raised from P1 by T9.24, right after P0, as the AI now serves the coach), T9.8 (Hard 2), T9.10 (Hard 3). **P1:** T9.25 (new in T9.24). **Moved to the Capstone:** T9.18 (T9.24). **Extras:** T9.9 (Medium 3, Hard 5), T8.5, T8.6.

**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): core 2 and 4, evaluation: core concepts (how agents and function calling work); Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): core 2 (tool calling), evaluation: core concepts (how tool calling is implemented, code organisation).

Done: nodes `guard`, `prepare` (the prompts, then the index and the clients, in the old order), `translate`, `retrieve`, `agent`, `tools` (`ToolNode`), `end_round` (the calls that are not JSON) and `finish`; the edge after the agent is our own `after_agent()` rather than `tools_condition`, as a reply whose only calls are not JSON is a round too (owner's choice of 2026-10-26). `tests/test_agent_graph.py`: the nodes, the edges, each node and each edge function, the output holding only the result, the longest turn's steps, the order of tool runs, the calls one at a time, the callbacks' thread.

Done, with `bind_tools`, `stream`, `batch` and the async methods covered too, `**kwargs` in those calls refused, assignments to a client's settings caught, and `ChatOpenAI`, `OpenAIEmbeddings` or `init_chat_model` outside `src/llm.py` caught; what an `ast` scan cannot see is in its docstring.

### T9.4 Short-term memory: a conversation that remembers earlier turns (M) · status: done · issue #99 (NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#7)
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): Medium 2; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): Easy 1 (conversation history).
**Depends on:** T9.2, O8
**Owner's choices (2026-10-30, Question phase):** the thread keeps each answered turn's question and answer only, never its tool calls, tool results or `<sources>` (so criteria 5 and 6 are worded for that below; before: "tool arguments with profile values" and "the cap falling inside a tool exchange"); each turn still needs its own basis (AC-2), so a follow-up resting on the conversation alone gets "not covered"; the stored answer drops its `[S#]` citations; the owner writes O8's T9.4 part from Claude's list of points.
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] The graph is compiled with an `InMemorySaver` for every user, guest or signed in; each browser session holds its own checkpointer and `thread_id` in `st.session_state` (never in `st.cache_resource`, so sessions share nothing and a closed session's memory goes with it); no conversation is written to disk - unit test: a second invoke on the same thread sees the first turn, a new thread does not; AppTest: two sessions do not see each other's threads; a test that no checkpoint file is created. Done: the conversation graph of `src/agent/memory.py` (`turn -> forget`) is compiled with each `Conversation`'s own `InMemorySaver`, held in `st.session_state["conversation"]`; `tests/test_agent_memory.py` (`test_a_second_turn_on_the_same_thread_sees_the_first`, `test_a_new_thread_does_not_see_the_first_turn`, `test_each_conversation_has_its_own_in_memory_checkpointer`, `test_no_checkpoint_file_is_created`), `tests/test_app_chat.py` (`test_two_sessions_do_not_see_each_others_conversation`, `test_the_conversation_is_never_a_cached_resource`).
- [x] A follow-up ("and for 200 g?") is answered from the earlier turn - test with a scripted fake model on the messages it receives, plus one live follow-up by the owner (O13). Done: `test_a_follow_up_is_answered_from_the_earlier_turn`; the owner's live check of 2026-10-30 (`docs/TESTING.md`, "Conversation memory (T9.4)"): "and for 200 g?" looked up 200 g of rolled oats, a meal plan's follow-up was checked as the same day, and the measured turn kept `HISTORY_MAX_TOKENS` at 2000.
- [x] The model sees at most the last `config.HISTORY_MAX_TOKENS` of the conversation (`trim_messages` in the node), while the stored thread keeps up to the cap below - unit test comparing the model's input with `get_state()`. Done: `chat.trim_history()` in the `prepare` node; `test_the_model_sees_only_the_latest_messages_that_fit_the_token_limit`, `test_the_trimmed_history_never_starts_with_an_answer`.
- [x] Every new message passes the guard first; a rejected message is not added to the thread - unit test, and a spy test: a message an existing domain guardrail catches (e.g. `Concern.MEDICAL`) followed by a normal question, asserting that no model input of the second turn holds any of the rejected text, and that the log holds none either. Whichever of T9.4 and T9.19a merges second adds the same test with a pain or injury message (AC-39). Done (T9.19a merged first, so both cases are here): `test_a_rejected_message_never_reaches_a_later_turn[medical]` and `[injury]` (the second turn's model and translator inputs, every log record and every checkpoint), `test_a_rejected_injection_leaves_the_thread_unchanged`, `tests/test_agent_graph.py::test_a_rejected_turn_gives_back_no_question`.
- [x] The `<profile>` block, tool arguments and tool results (some hold profile values) and the `<sources>` blocks never enter the thread; only the current turn's model calls get them - test that reads the latest checkpoint and `get_state_history()` for the canary profile values. The one exception is a save proposal waiting for the user's answer (T9.7): T9.7 replaces its arguments in the thread's latest state once the user answers, while the earlier in-memory checkpoints keep them until "New chat" or the end of the session (never on disk) - test on `get_state()` after every choice (with T9.7). Done: the turn's graph is compiled with `checkpointer=False`, and the thread keeps only the question and the answer; `test_no_profile_value_tool_result_or_source_is_remembered` (canary weight, height and TDEE, `weight_kg`, `<sources>` and every fixture chunk, over `get_state_history()` and the saver's storage, with the control that the turn's model got them), `test_the_thread_keeps_the_question_and_the_answer_without_citations`, `test_a_turn_that_is_not_answered_is_not_kept`, `tests/test_agent_graph.py::test_the_turn_graph_is_never_checkpointed`. T9.7's part is T9.7's.
- [x] The thread's state keeps at most `config.HISTORY_MAX_MESSAGES` messages: a node deletes older ones with `RemoveMessage` (what is stored), next to `trim_messages` (what the model sees), and cuts only before a user message, so no answer is left without the question it answers; older in-memory checkpoints keep removed messages until "New chat" - unit tests on `get_state()` after more turns than the cap, and with the cap falling between a question and its answer. Done: the `forget` node and `memory.first_kept()`; `test_the_thread_keeps_at_most_the_cap`, `test_the_cap_never_cuts_between_a_question_and_its_answer`, `test_first_kept_starts_on_a_question`.
- [x] A "New chat" button discards any pending proposal (T9.7), deletes the old thread from the checkpointer (`delete_thread()`), starts a new `thread_id` and clears the chat; the summary PDF and the session cost cover the current chat - unit test: the old thread has no checkpoint left (`get_state_history()` empty); AppTest (owner's choice of 2026-10-25, after the code review of T9.1). Done (no proposal exists before T9.7): `Conversation.new_chat()` and the Ask tab's button; `test_new_chat_deletes_the_old_thread`; AppTest `test_new_chat_clears_the_chat_and_forgets_the_conversation`, `test_new_chat_is_off_until_there_is_a_chat`, `test_new_chat_keeps_the_rate_limit`, `tests/test_app_export.py::test_after_new_chat_the_pdf_holds_only_the_new_chat`, `tests/test_app_usage.py::test_after_new_chat_the_session_total_counts_the_new_chat_only`.
- [x] The answer prompt no longer says each request stands alone, and its Safety section names pain and injury (O8, written by the owner; T9.19a's second line of defence) - owner review; a test on the built prompt, and `test_the_answer_prompt_keeps_the_domain_guardrails` gains the pain and injury phrase. Done: the owner's O8 text of 2026-10-30, with Claude's wording fix on where earlier messages sit (owner's choice); `test_the_built_prompt_no_longer_says_each_request_stands_alone` (the two sentences gone, four of the owner's conversation rules present, English and German) and the guardrail test's pain, injury and 112 phrases (the Safety lines came with T9.20).
**Notes:** cost rises with history; the trim limit is set from a measured turn. Serves AC-31. The T9.2 note below is settled by not checkpointing the turn's graph at all: the conversation graph around it keeps only messages, so no per-turn key carries over (T9.4). Left by T9.2 (its code review): the graph's state is built for one invocation per turn. With a checkpointer and a `thread_id` the keys of the last turn stay: `after_guard()` and `after_retrieve()` route on `"result" in state`, so the next turn would end at once with the old result; `rounds`, `answer` and `runs` (an `operator.add` list that cannot be cleared) carry over; and `text` and `profile` would be checkpointed. Route on a value each turn writes (or `Command(goto=...)`), reset the per-turn keys in the guard node, give `runs` a reducer that can be cleared, and keep the profile in `TurnContext` (it is already there through the tools) rather than in state, which also serves this ticket's `<profile>` criterion.

### T9.6 The SQLite store and the authorisation layer (L) · status: done · issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#9 (the copy of #101)
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): Medium 4 (personalisation; T9.5 adds the authentication), Medium 2 in part (the store T9.7's long-term memory writes to), core 4 (real-world use), evaluation: appropriate security; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): Medium 4 (personalisation), evaluation: technical (appropriate security); beyond the brief (owner's request): the coach-client sharing model.
**Depends on:** T9.1, T8.2 (the canary log tests)
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `src/store/` uses the standard library's `sqlite3` on a file in a gitignored directory set in config (D13); a versioned migration function creates the tables of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 16, with foreign keys on, non-negative quantities checked and UTC timestamps (D21) - unit tests on a temporary database. Done: `config.STORE_DIR` (`data/store/`, gitignored) and `config.STORE_PATH`; `db.connect()` turns foreign keys and `secure_delete` on, keeps temporary data in memory (`temp_store`) and, on POSIX, makes the app's folder and the file owner-only and runs `db.migrate()`, which applies `schema.MIGRATIONS` by `user_version` in one transaction and refuses a newer file; 15 STRICT tables (section 16, with the changes recorded there: `audit_log.subject_id`, `meal_items.consumed_on` and `food_name`, `ai_reviews.period_start`); CHECKs on every quantity, role, scope and status, and on every timestamp (UTC, `+00:00`) and day (ISO). `tests/test_store_db.py`; `tests/conftest.py` points every test at a temporary file.
- [x] Every query is parameterised - a scan test fails on SQL built with f-strings, `%`, `.format()` or `+` in `src/store/`. Done: `tests/test_store_sql_scan.py` refuses any string built at run time in `src/store/` (f-strings, `%`, `+`, `+=`, `.format()`, `.join()` of SQL) and any `execute*` whose query is not a literal or a module constant (migrations from `schema.MIGRATIONS` allowed and checked), with cases it must catch and cases it must pass.
- [x] Every read of another user's data takes the acting user and checks the role, an active relationship and the scope; a missing, foreign, ended or unshared resource gives the same `NotFound` (D16) - cross-client tests for every read function (AC-21, AC-22). Done: `access.authorised_read()`; scopes as the owner chose (2026-10-27): body = weights and profile entries, nutrition = meals, targets and the coach's own reviews, training = workouts, preferences never shared; a coach's own list and export of their reviews keep only clients who still share nutrition (one audit row per client read), and a gymer never gets the model's draft (owner's choices after the code review). `tests/test_store_access.py` also fails on any public store function that is not classified as a tested read, own-only, a write or an internal. `tests/test_store_access.py`: every `list_*` function (a test fails if one is added without being listed) against ten refused cases, a scope off while the others are on, revoking and ending (AC-22); the same `NotFound` and no audit row each time.
- [x] Nutrient snapshots and profile entries are never changed in place, and a new target is a new row - unit tests. Done: a trigger refuses any UPDATE on the record tables and the audit log, and on a decided review; the modules have no update function. Beyond the criterion (from the reviews): the store also keeps the profile form's ranges (adults only), the calorie floor on every target and records for gymers only. `tests/test_store_records.py` (snapshots, profile entries, targets) and `tests/test_store_db.py` (every record table).
- [x] A coach's read of client data and every review decision write an audit row with no values - unit tests. Done: `access.audit()` writes IDs, the action, the kind of data and the time only; a coach's read writes it in the read's transaction (the coach's own review list and export write one per client read), `reviews.decide_review()` in the decision's. `tests/test_store_access.py`, `tests/test_store_reviews.py`. A refused read is not audited (owner's choice, 2026-10-27).
- [x] `export_user_data()` returns all of a user's rows as JSON; `delete_user_data()` removes them, the user's relationships, the reviews about the user and those the user wrote as a coach, and the audit rows by or about the user (the app is local and keeps no audit duty; the owner may change this) - unit tests with foreign keys on (AC-34); T9.16 adds the review threads. Done: `src/store/my_data.py`; a gymer's export holds the approved reviews about them without the model's draft, a coach's the reviews they wrote about clients who still share nutrition (what each reads); the owner accepts a gymer's export without pending or rejected reviews as meeting AC-34 (2026-10-27). `tests/test_store_my_data.py`: no column names the user after the delete, `foreign_key_check` is clean, other users' rows stay, and a table the delete forgets makes it fail with nothing changed.
- [x] No stored value appears in a log record - T8.2's canary tests extended to the store (AC-33). Done: `tests/test_observability.py::test_no_record_or_error_holds_a_stored_value` runs every store function with canary values at DEBUG and checks every log line and every error's text; the store writes no log line.
- [x] `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md`, `docs/PRD.md` (feature 4 and the privacy note) and `docs/SECURITY.md` (what is stored, where, for whom and how it is deleted; a shared machine, a copied file; the file is not encrypted; local only) are updated - owner review. Approved by the owner on 2026-10-28 (after the compliance review and its fixes), then corrected after the final compliance and security runs on 608bb83 and code-review rounds 7-11 (who sees the relationship, its audit rows now named as the coach's reads and review decisions; who sees which display name, and the T9.12 exception for it; what a gymer reads of a review; the rollback journal; `temp_store`; what the sharing pattern check covers; the SQL scan's coverage, the refusal of text that cannot be stored and no error kept as context; the PRD's "shares a gymer's records and reviews"); and the owner confirmed the corrected sentences on 2026-10-28: the rule's "Personal data" section, PRD feature 4 and "The privacy change", and in `docs/SECURITY.md` an asset, three threat rows (reads by someone not shared with, SQL injection, data on disk) and the known limits (the store trusts the acting user it is given; permissions, the rollback journal, re-sharing a scope).
**Notes:** no UI here: T9.5 adds the first screens. Was "Long-term memory: the store and the My data view"; reworked by T9.1 for the coach-first scope, and moved before T9.5 so sign-in has a store to write to. PostgreSQL replaces this module in the Capstone.

### T9.12 Invitations and sharing (M) · status: done · issue #112 (NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#15)
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): core 2 (user interactions), core 3, evaluation: appropriate security; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): none; beyond the brief (owner's request): the coach-client relationship.
**Depends on:** T9.5
**Owner's choices (2026-11-03, Question phase):** a gymer has one coach at a time (a code is refused while the gymer has an active coach; the PRD's feature 15 says so); a coach's read of a client's display name is not audited (the name is part of the relationship, which both sides see in their exports; so T9.7's client-name check reads names unaudited). Defaults stated and kept: 12 characters from 32 that are hard to mix up (`INVITE_CODE_LENGTH`, `INVITE_CODE_ALPHABET`), SHA-256 in the store, `INVITE_TTL_DAYS` = 7, `INVITE_MAX_ATTEMPTS` = 5 a day in Berlin, every try counted (a valid one too) before the code is checked; the new code kept in the coach's session until "I have noted the code"; cancel deletes the row; every scope off until the gymer ticks it; the pages Clients (after Chat) and Coach and sharing (after My profile), the homes unchanged; the gymer's page says the app does not check a coach's qualifications (T9.5's compliance note); an `invite_refused` log line with the reason only.
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] A coach creates an invitation: a random code from the `secrets` module (length from config), shown once, stored as a hash, expiring after `config.INVITE_TTL_DAYS`; the coach can cancel it - unit tests and AppTest (D15). Done: `src/store/invitations.py` (`create_invitation()`, `list_invitations()` without the code, `cancel_invitation()`); `tests/test_store_invitations.py` (length and alphabet, `secrets.choice` for every character, only the hash stored, the expiry, cancel by the owner only); `tests/test_app_sharing.py` (`test_a_new_code_is_shown_once_and_stored_only_as_its_hash`, `test_the_coach_cancels_a_code`).
- [x] A gymer enters the code and turns each scope on or off (nutrition, training, body); a valid, unexpired, unused code makes the relationship active; an invalid, used or expired code gets one fixed message that does not say which - unit tests (AC-20). Done: `invitations.accept_invitation()` (one transaction: the code marked used, the relationship started, the ticked scopes granted); `tests/test_store_invitations.py` (each set of scopes, hyphens, spaces and case, the refused cases: expired, used, cancelled, never created, malformed, too long, not text; one coach at a time); AppTest `test_a_valid_code_connects_the_gymer_with_the_chosen_scopes`, `test_every_bad_code_gets_the_one_fixed_message`.
- [x] Code attempts are limited per user key in the store (`config.INVITE_MAX_ATTEMPTS` a day), so a page reload does not reset them - unit test. Done: `invite_attempts`, counted and committed before the code is checked; `test_every_try_counts_and_the_limit_refuses_even_a_valid_code`, `test_the_count_survives_a_new_connection`, `test_the_limit_is_per_gymer_and_per_day_in_berlin`, `test_a_failed_accept_rolls_back_but_keeps_the_count`; AppTest `test_the_daily_limit_holds_across_a_reload`.
- [x] A "Coach and sharing" page lets the gymer change scopes, revoke and end the relationship; the coach's next read is blocked - AppTest and store tests (AC-22). Done: `views/sharing.py`; AppTest `test_turning_a_scope_off_blocks_the_coachs_next_read`, `test_ending_the_relationship_needs_a_tick_and_blocks_every_read`, `test_a_relationship_ended_by_the_coach_meanwhile_is_shown_as_it_is`, `test_a_failed_save_shows_the_stored_scopes_again` (the scopes saved in one transaction, `users.set_scopes()`); the store tests of T9.6 (`tests/test_store_access.py`, revoking and ending).
- [x] The coach's client list shows each client's display name and which scopes are on, the name read through the same function as the gymer's below - AppTest. Done: `views/clients.py` and `users.list_relationships()`; AppTest `test_the_coach_sees_each_client_with_the_scopes_on`, `test_the_client_list_reads_names_through_the_relationship_checked_function`; `tests/test_store_access.py::test_the_relationship_lists_read_names_through_display_name_of`.
- [x] The gymer's "Coach and sharing" page shows their coach's display name, read through a function that checks the relationship (never `users.find_user()`, which checks no access), so the gymer knows who sees their data (owner's choice, 2026-10-28, from T9.6's code review). That function is the one exception to the rule that every read of another user's data goes through `access.authorised_read()`, and it gets its own group in `tests/test_store_access.py`: allowed for both sides of an active or ended relationship; refused for a missing user, a stranger, another coach's client and an ID that is not text; so a gymer does not see a coach's name before accepting a code; whether a coach's name read is audited is decided in this ticket - AppTest and the store test. Done: `users.display_name_of()`, not audited (owner's choice, 2026-11-03); `tests/test_store_access.py` group `NAME_READS` (allowed for both sides of an active or ended relationship, refused for a missing user or actor, strangers, another coach's client, a coach and a coach, oneself and IDs that are not text, no audit row either way; `test_a_gymer_does_not_see_a_coachs_name_before_accepting`); AppTest `test_a_gymer_with_no_coach_sees_the_code_form_with_every_scope_off` and the coach's name after accepting.
- [x] The owner accepts an invitation with a second Google account - owner live check (O13). Done: the owner's check of 2026-11-03 on f3e5af0 with two Google accounts (a coach and a gymer in a private window): a code created on Clients and accepted on Coach and sharing with scopes ticked, the coach's list showing the gymer and the scopes, then Delete everything; the owner reported that everything worked (the guide's optional steps, a scope off and the end, not confirmed separately; `docs/TESTING.md`, Live checks).
**Notes:** no email is sent; the coach passes the code on by hand. Owner's choices after compliance run 1 (2026-11-03): its two Lows fixed in the PR (the texts say the coach always sees the display name and keeps the name and dates after the end; the nutrition scope's reviews are drafted by an AI model); the gymer learns the coach's name on accepting, in one step, and a former client's name stays in the coach's list until an account is deleted; naming the coach before any scope opens, retention and explicit consent go to the Capstone.

Done: `metrics.week_metrics(meals, weights, targets, monday)` takes no ID; `diary.read_week(conn, actor_id, subject_id, monday)` reads the records through the store's access checks for T9.15 and T9.16; `tests/test_metrics.py`, `test_read_week_gives_the_metrics_of_the_stored_records`, `test_read_week_goes_through_the_access_checks`.

### T9.14 Workout log and the weekly training metrics (M) · status: done · issue #114 (NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#17)
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): core 2, core 3; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): none; beyond the brief (owner's request): training tracking.
**Depends on:** T9.5 (O8 dropped on 2026-10-26: the chat's training rules are T9.20's)
**Owner's choices (2026-11-03, Question phase):** the plan's criteria stand over the issue's older text (its fourth criterion still named O8 and AC-35, dropped on 2026-10-26); 24 exercises in six muscle groups, named in English and German; ranges reps 1-100, 0-500 kg (0 for a body-weight set with no added weight), RPE 1-10 in steps of 0.5 and optional, at most 40 sets a workout, no future day; weights typed and shown in the user's units and stored in kg; the Workouts page shows the week's totals per exercise; T9.14 creates `src/metrics.py` (T9.13, worked on at the same time, adds the nutrition metrics to it).
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `data/exercises.json` holds a short list of exercise names with their muscle group, written by us (no third-party list) - review; a test loads and checks it. Done: 24 exercises in the six groups of `config.MUSCLE_GROUPS`, each with an ID and a name in English and German, and a note that we wrote it; `src/exercises.py` reads and checks it (Pydantic, unique IDs, a known group, a short one-line name per language); `tests/test_exercises.py`.
- [x] A session has a date and sets (exercise, reps, kg and an optional RPE, ranges from config); sessions can be edited and deleted - unit tests and AppTest (AC-24). Done: the store accepts only an exercise of the list and `config.WORKOUT_REPS_RANGE`, `WORKOUT_WEIGHT_KG_RANGE`, `WORKOUT_RPE_RANGE` with `WORKOUT_RPE_STEP`, and at most `WORKOUT_MAX_SETS` sets (`records._checked_sets()`), and no day after today in Berlin (code review round 1); an edit is `records.replace_workout_session()`, the delete and the new rows in one transaction (records are never updated in place), classified in `tests/test_store_access.py` as a write; a gymer's Workouts page (`views/workouts.py`): the day, one set's fields, the draft's sets, Save; the week's workouts with Edit and Delete. `tests/test_store_records.py` (the refused sets, the ends of the ranges, the set limit, an edit, a failed edit, another user's or a missing workout), `tests/test_app_workouts.py` (logged and shown with its volume, a body-weight set, pounds stored as kg, remove the last set, edit, cancel, delete, the week picker, German, a store error on save, no page for a coach, an edit's day kept across a page change, an RPE put on the scale's step, no future day in the field, a past day picked and saved, an edit moved to another day, Edit off while a draft is unsaved, an emptied day field, no save without a saved profile, the link to My data). As for the goal control (T9.5), a workout is saved only once the gymer has saved a profile, so the first-save tick comes first (compliance run 1, owner's choice, 2026-11-03).
- [x] `src/metrics.py` adds the week's sessions, sets, volume (reps × kg) per exercise and top weight per exercise - known-answer tests. Done: `metrics.training_week()` (with `week_start()`, `week_days()`, `session_volume()`); `tests/test_metrics.py` (a week worked out by hand, workouts on the Sunday before and the Monday after left out, an empty week, the list's order, the weeks around the change from summer time and a year's end).
- [x] The training views show facts only, no advice: a test scans their texts in `src/i18n.py` for the advice words listed in config - unit tests. (Changed on 2026-10-26: the chat's training rules moved to T9.20, which may give cited training principles; the views and the log stay facts only.) Done: `config.TRAINING_ADVICE_WORDS` (English and German, whole words or phrases, `*` for a prefix); `tests/test_i18n.py::test_the_training_views_give_no_advice` scans every `workouts.` and `muscle.` text, the page's title, My data's volume column and the exercise names in both languages, with planted advice it must catch and facts it must let through.
**Notes:** the store's exercise check means a set always names an exercise of the list; an ID later taken off the list is still shown (by its ID), but a workout holding it cannot be saved again as it is: an edit's Save is refused ("could not be saved") until that set is removed, so whoever takes an exercise off the list should keep its ID or move its sets first (code review round 3). Volume is reps × kg as the criterion says, so a body-weight set at 0 kg adds none (the page says so). SQLite may give an edited workout the deleted one's ID (no `AUTOINCREMENT`). Demo Sam has two made-up workouts (Sam does not share training, so T9.15 shows the coach "not shared").

### T9.7 Long-term memory for the coach: `remember_preference` with the coach's approval (M) · status: done · issue #102 (NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#10)
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): Medium 2 (long-term memory, the course's "remember" tool pattern), Medium 4 in part (personalisation of the coach's chat), core 2 (user interactions) and the course's human-in-the-loop pattern; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): Medium 4 in part (personalisation of the coach's chat).
**Depends on:** T9.4 (the checkpointer that pauses, and "New chat"), T9.5, T9.12 (the relationship-checked read of a client's display name, which the client-name check uses), O8
**Owner's choices (2026-11-03, Question phase):** the weekly review's prompt does not take the preferences (D17 drafts the same way for every coach); the model answers and the card shows under the answer; after a round of proposals alone the model is asked once more, so a request that also asks something is answered, and a turn with no basis then ends with a fixed line instead of "not covered" (AC-2 holds; one more model call for a request that only asks to remember; owner's choice after code review round 1), which ends with a reminder to ask anything else again on its own (owner's choice after code review round 2); the chat input is locked while a proposal waits; code first with a placeholder for the coach's prompt part (`src/prompts/answer_coach.md`, joined in a coach's chat only), which the owner writes before the PR merges (O8). Defaults Claude stated and kept: after Save or Discard a fixed line in the chosen language, no further model call; at most one proposal a turn; a refused text shows no card and the model gets a fixed message per reason; names matched as whole words without case, no accent folding; `PREFERENCE_MAX_CHARS` 200, `MEMORY_PREFERENCES_IN_PROMPT` 20 (the newest), no cap on stored preferences; the checks do not count against the rate limit; the log events carry codes only.

### T9.21 Checks: the manifest's authors against the article, and the evaluated code's git state (S) · status: done · issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#44
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): the Sprint 2 review, point 5 (evaluation results the development loop can trust), core 4 (an app that copes with real-world use), evaluation: technical (a relevant knowledge base); Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): core 1 (the knowledge base), Hard 7 (RAG evaluation), evaluation: technical (a relevant knowledge base).
**Depends on:** T2.1 (the download and its licence and DOI check), T8.4 (the results file); came out of T9.19's reviews (PR NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#43, owner's choice, 2026-10-29), but needs none of its code.
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] `check_article()` (`src/rag/sources.py`, used by `scripts/download_sources.py` and the ingest) refuses an article whose authors differ from the manifest entry's `authors`, as it refuses a licence or DOI mismatch: the download reports it as `REFUSED`, writes no file and deletes an older copy; the ingest refuses it (`IngestError`). The article's authors are the contributors in `front/article-meta/contrib-group`, in order, leaving out a contributor in a group with `content-type="editor"`, one with a `contrib-type` other than `author`, and one with a `<role>` that names an editor as a word (MDPI's "Academic Editor"; a CRediT role such as "Writing – review & editing" is not one). A name is `given-names` then `surname`, compared without case, dots and repeated spaces (so "Alan A. Aragon" matches "Alan A Aragon"); a group author is its `<collab>` text. The reason names the authors missing on each side, or says the order differs - unit tests on made-up XML (no real article text): match; dotted initials; an extra author; a missing one; another order; an MDPI-style editor group with an "Academic Editor" role left out; a CRediT "review & editing" author kept; a `contrib-type="editor"` left out; no author at all refused; a collab author. Done: `tests/test_sources_license.py` (the author cases), `tests/test_download_sources.py::test_an_author_mismatch_is_refused_and_an_older_copy_deleted`, `tests/test_ingest.py::test_a_file_whose_authors_differ_from_the_manifest_stops_the_build`; also Unicode NFC forms, a contributor outside the article meta, a nested collab member list, `name-alternatives`, `string-name`, a nameless contributor and control characters in a name.
- [x] Every existing XML fixture and inline test article that reaches `check_article()` names its made-up authors, and the ingest and download tests pass with the check on - `python -m pytest -q`. Done: `cc_by.xml`, `ingest_article.xml` and the inline article of `tests/test_sources_license.py` name their made-up authors (the refused-licence fixtures stop before the author check); the full suite passes (4008 passed, 9 skipped).
- [x] Before merging, the check is run offline on the owner's downloaded articles (gitignored `data/raw/`, no network) with the manifest of `main` and, if T9.19 has not merged yet, T9.19's: all match - manual check, recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md` (a prototype of the rule matched all 31 on 2026-10-29). Done (2026-10-29): `main`'s manifest 21 of 21, T9.19's 31 of 31 (its topic validation skipped, as `main` lacks `resistance_training`); a made-up editor added back to #25 or #33 is refused - `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
- [x] `scripts/run_evaluation.py` reads the git state when the run starts (before the first question) and again when it writes the results file; the file's `commit` and `dirty` are the start's, and three added fields, `commit_at_end`, `dirty_at_end` and `changed_during_run` (true when HEAD or the tracked files' content changed between the two reads, null when git could not say either time), record the end; the content is compared by a hash of `git diff HEAD`, so an edit to a file that was already changed counts - unit tests with a fake git runner (no change, a new commit, an edited tracked file, git unavailable) and a test in a temporary real repository. Done: `tests/test_eval_results.py` (fake runner: the diff hash, a failing diff that keeps the commit, the three calls; the four cases of `changed_during_run`; a real repository: an edit, a second edit to a changed file, a restore, a commit), `tests/test_run_evaluation.py::test_a_commit_made_during_the_run_is_not_recorded_as_the_code_that_ran` (the first read before the answer model is called).
- [x] When `changed_during_run` is true, the printed summary says so with both short commits, and `scripts/check_eval_baseline.py` notes it next to that file's commit; the exit codes do not change - unit tests on the printed lines and `compare()`'s lines. Done: `tests/test_eval_results.py::test_the_change_note_names_both_commits_or_the_edited_files`, `tests/test_run_evaluation.py` (the note printed, none when nothing changed, exit 0 both times), `tests/test_check_eval_baseline.py::test_a_run_whose_code_changed_during_it_is_noted_next_to_its_commit`.
- [x] `EVAL_RESULTS_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1, as fields are only added (its comment says a rename or removal raises it): the committed `data/eval_baseline.json`, which lacks the new fields, still compares with a new results file - `tests/test_check_eval_baseline.py`. Done: `tests/test_check_eval_baseline.py::test_the_committed_baseline_compares_with_a_file_of_the_new_layout`; the comment on `EVAL_RESULTS_SCHEMA_VERSION` already says when it is raised.
- [x] `docs/TESTING.md` (the results file's fields), `README.md` or `CLAUDE.md` where they describe the two scripts, and `docs/PROGRESS.md` are updated - review; `code-reviewer` and `compliance-reviewer` (the download code is covered by its rule) on the PR. Done: `docs/TESTING.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`; `README.md` and `CLAUDE.md` name the scripts but not these fields, so they are unchanged. The `code-reviewer` rounds and the `compliance-reviewer` (5421a3e: no open findings) on NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#45, recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
**Notes:** the check would have caught T9.19's two editors (#25, #33), which a reviewer found by reading the XML. Author names are the articles' public bibliographic data, already in `data/sources.json` and `ATTRIBUTION.md`; a refusal prints them like a DOI. The diff is hashed in memory and never written. ### The owner's UI decisions of 2026-10-30 (T9.22, T9.23, and T9.15's phone cards)

Made by the owner while reviewing the T9.15 dashboard mockups on a Claude Design canvas (outside the repo, so this section carries them): (1) the app is renamed "Fitcoach AI Assistant"; (2) the language control moves from the header to the sidebar, labelled "Language", with the options "English" and "Deutsch" and no icon; (3) a custom CSS layer is allowed for the whole app: one fixed stylesheet in the repo, loaded with `st.html`, aimed at the classes `st.container(key=...)` gives (`st-key-<key>`) and a few stable test ids, never a CDN or any external CSS (visitors' IPs stay off third-party hosts, as with the fonts); this replaces the "native first" principle of `docs/UI_DESIGN.md` and widens the single `st.html` exception in `docs/SECURITY.md`; (4) a brand sidebar: a deep green background with light text through the sidebar theme of `.streamlit/config.toml`, and `st.logo`; (5) on phones the coach's client list shows as cards, while tablets and desktops keep the table (a CSS media query, as Streamlit cannot read the screen width). The owner's answers of the same day, after the draft: a week in progress says "4 of 7 days logged" (AC-26's wording, unchanged); the German UI keeps the same name, the language label is "Sprache" in German, the PDF's file name stays, the stylesheet sets no colours, and the cut order below stands. The planned look is described in `docs/UI_DESIGN.md`, "Planned: the owner's UI decisions of 2026-10-30".

Risk: a Streamlit upgrade may change the classes the test ids sit on; the rules then stop applying and the page falls back to Streamlit's own layout.

**Notes:** since T9.2 a turn is a LangGraph run: the root run's input is the raw text (even one the guard rejects, which made no LangChain run before) and the profile, and every node's state update is a run output, so the masking must cover the graph's own inputs and outputs, not only the model calls (found by T9.2's reviews); since T9.4 that includes the turn graph's `history` input and the conversation graph's messages, whose kept answers may restate profile values (T9.4's compliance review). Never add a LangGraph `RetryPolicy` without a check: LangGraph then logs the error's message and traceback through its own logger, outside the app's formatter. Owner's choices in the Question phase (2026-11-06): the EU region; the live check recorded in words; T9.17 adds its tools to the tracing tests; the PR merges after the owner's live check.

### T9.10 Evaluation report with Ragas or DeepEval (M, should) · status: done · issue #105 (NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#13)
