- [x] The library (Ragas or DeepEval, chosen in the Research phase) is pinned after its licence is checked (rule 9); its judge model is built with `build_chat_model()` and any embeddings with `build_embeddings()`, both from the allowlist, and the library is given no model, token or URL override - unit tests, and T9.2's client-rule scan covers the script. Done: `deepeval==4.3.3` in the `eval` extra (Apache-2.0, read in its `LICENSE.md` and metadata; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` section 12; its packages walked by `tests/test_dependency_licences.py`); `config.EVAL_JUDGE_MODEL` built by `build_chat_model()` (`test_score_writes_each_question_s_scores_into_the_results_file`, `test_the_judge_model_is_on_the_allowlist`); the `Judge` wrapper passes nothing but the reply's schema (`test_the_judge_asks_the_built_client_for_the_schema_and_nothing_else`); the two metrics need no embeddings; `tests/test_client_rules.py` names `src/eval_scoring.py` and `scripts/run_evaluation.py`.
- [x] A script scores the owner's evaluation questions (O2) on faithfulness, answer relevancy and, if the owner writes reference answers (O14), context precision and recall, from the agent's answers and retrieved chunks, and writes the scores per question into `results.json` - unit tests with a mocked judge. Done: `scripts/run_evaluation.py --score`, faithfulness and answer relevancy into the run's results file (`run-<date>-<time>.json`), per run and as means; O14 not written, so no context precision or recall (owner's choice, 2026-11-06) - `tests/test_eval_scoring.py`, `tests/test_run_evaluation.py`.
- [x] A report in `docs/TESTING.md` gives the scores, the cost of the run, what they show and their limits (an LLM judging an LLM, a small question set) - owner live run (O13) and review. Done: "Evaluation scores with DeepEval (T9.10)", the owner's run of 2026-11-06 (faithfulness 1.0000 over 12 answers, answer relevancy 0.9787 over 15; $0.0308 for the answers and $0.1188 for the judge, the script's figures, as the course's Tracker did not move); what they show (faithfulness here means no claim contradicted and checks no citation, q12; relevancy scores q11's correct "not covered" answer low) and the limits; the owner's review of the report is the PR's.
**Notes:** the owner's choices in the Question phase (2026-11-06): DeepEval, as Ragas 0.4.3 cannot be installed with the `openai` pin (its `instructor` needs `jiter<0.15`, `openai` 3.35.1 `jiter>=0.16`); only faithfulness and answer relevancy until O14 is written; the judge was to be `openai/gpt-5`, a stronger model than the answers' `openai/gpt-5-mini`, so that no model grades its own answers; the course's OpenRouter key does not allow it (404 on every judge call of the first scored run, no token billed), so the judge is `openai/gpt-5-mini` (owner's choice, 2026-11-06), and grading its own answers is among the limits in `docs/TESTING.md`. DeepEval's telemetry, `.env` reading and local run store are switched off and a Confident AI key stops the run (`docs/SECURITY.md`). The issue was copied to this repository as NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#13.

the follow-up flag counts the current week as the criterion says, early-week flags being a known limit (owner's choice, the same day) (`records.add_profile_with_target()` returns False); once the gymer has no coach, a Save writes its target, which replaces the coach's; the gymer's page says "set by your coach" without naming the coach; O15's coach assistant part is built first with the tool descriptions, and the owner writes it in `src/prompts/answer_coach.md` before the merge (as T9.16 did). ### T9.9 Food lookup through the USDA FoodData Central API (M, extra) · status: done · issue #104 (NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#12)
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): Medium 3 and Hard 5; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): none; T6.1 known problem 11 (foods missing from the Foundation Foods table).
**Depends on:** T9.2, O12, the owner's approval of a copyright policy change
**Acceptance criteria**
- [x] Before any code: the FoodData Central API's data licence and terms are read on USDA's own pages and recorded in `docs/SOURCES.md`, and the owner approves the change to copyright policy rule 10, which today says USDA data comes from its download page - owner approval recorded in this plan. Done: USDA's home page and API guide, and api.data.gov's developer manual, read 2026-11-06 and recorded in row #21 (CC0 1.0 for FoodData Central data, USDA's suggested citation, 1,000 requests an hour, no other terms; CC0 not confirmed for Branded and Experimental foods, so they are excluded). **The owner approved the change to rule 10 on 2026-11-06** as proposed in the Question phase (a food the user asks about may come from the API with the owner's key: one search per lookup, only the food name sent, never a bulk download or a mirror of the database), and chose the same day the data types (Foundation, SR Legacy, Survey (FNDDS)) and the scope (the chat's food tool only: not the meal plan check, the diary or the Food lookup tab).
- [x] When the local table has no good match, the food tool asks the API; the API key follows the same rules as the OpenRouter key; calls have a timeout, bounded retries and mapped errors; the result names its source ("USDA FoodData Central API") - unit tests with a mocked HTTP client (no network in tests). Done: "no good match" is the table's `not_found` (Question phase default); `tests/test_food_api.py` (the request, the header, timeouts, one retry for a timeout, a connection error or a 5xx, no retry for 429, 401, 403 or another 4xx, bad answers, the key from secrets or the environment, `source` and `origin`), `tests/test_tools_registry.py` (only a missing food asks, six searches a turn, the meal plan never asks), `tests/test_observability.py` (the `food_api` line holds no name or key); `tests/conftest.py` fails any test that sends a request. Not verified: a real answer, which needs the key (O12, O13).
- [x] The food name sent to the API is validated, and only the food name is sent - unit test on the request. Done: `test_the_request_body_holds_only_the_name_and_the_fixed_fields`, `test_other_names_are_not_sent`, `test_a_refused_name_makes_no_request`.
- [x] `ATTRIBUTION.md` and the tool results credit the API data as its terms require - review. Done: USDA's suggested citation under "Food data" in `ATTRIBUTION.md` (`test_the_food_data_credits_the_fooddata_central_api_as_usda_asks`), the result's `source`, the line under the values in the app and the PDF (`test_a_food_from_the_api_shows_the_apis_source_line`, `test_a_food_from_the_api_names_the_api_and_is_credited`) and the PDF's credits.
**Notes:** an extra, done only if time allows. Done on 2026-11-06 at the owner's request, ahead of its place in the order (after T9.17, T9.8, T9.10 and T9.25). The live check with the key (a food missing from the table, e.g. olive oil or tofu; the answer's shape and the `X-Api-Key` header) is in O13, once O12's key exists.

### T9.26 Capstone plan: the gymer's own preferences shared with the coach, and gymer-drafted meal plans (S) · status: done · issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#67
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): evaluation: reflection (suggestions for improving the project), in part; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): none; beyond the brief (owner's request): the Capstone design itself.
**Depends on:** T9.24
**Acceptance criteria** (approved by the owner, 2026-11-04)
- [x] `docs/PRD.md`, "Moved to the Capstone", records both ideas: (a) the gymer's own preferences (e.g. "vegetarian", "I like to draft my own meal plans") saved by the gymer with the gymer's approval, never written by the coach or the coach's model about a client, shared with the coach only through a new opt-in `preferences` scope (off by default, revoked like the other scopes), and read by the coach's agent through a client tool as delimited data under the client's opaque reference, never by name; (b) a gymer drafts a meal plan, the coach approves, edits or rejects it (the weekly review's human-in-the-loop pattern), and the gymer sees the draft and the coach's decision; with the conditions before any real user (explicit Art. 9 consent apart from the sharing scopes; the gymer sees, exports and deletes them, AC-34) - review. Done: three bullets after the "Moved to the Capstone" paragraph; from code review round 1 they also plan T9.7's checks on every saved preference (input guard, domain guardrails, the gymer's own display name) and the coach's approval before a write tool acts on one, the consent's naming of the AI model and its provider, and which meal-plan questions are left to the Capstone; the PRD's list of flows to a model (Guardrails added in Sprint 3) points to them too; from compliance run 1, the consent's withdrawal and the gymer's-chat path, the beliefs a diet can reveal, the warning on the form or card and a length limit, and a pointer from the "aggregates" guardrail.
- [x] Sprint 3 stays as it is: feature 22, AC-36 and the privacy section's "Preferences are never shared with a coach" still hold for Sprint 3; that line only points to the Capstone plan - review; `git diff` touches nothing in `src/`, `views/`, `tests/` or `app.py`. Done: feature 22 and AC-36 unchanged; the privacy line gains a pointer; `git diff --stat main` lists only the three docs files.
- [x] `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` has T9.26 (a row in the order table and a section) with its status, and `docs/PROGRESS.md` has its entry - review. Done: the row, this section, the plan's status line and the PROGRESS entry.
- [x] `code-reviewer` runs on the PR and its report is posted on it; `compliance-reviewer` runs too, as the text plans health data shared with a coach and sent to a model (`.claude/rules/workflow.md`: when unsure, run it) - their reports on the PR. Done: code review rounds on NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#68 (`docs/PROGRESS.md`, T9.26); compliance run 1 on cc7aa9c, four Lows, all fixed in the PR (owner's choice, 2026-11-04).
**Notes:** the decisions compliance run 1 left to the owner stay open for the Capstone: one choice or two in the `preferences` scope (sharing with the coach, and sending to a model); preferences entered by form only, or by the gymer's chat too; reusing the `preferences` table (coaches only under T9.7) or a gymer table, either way with a migration of the scope CHECK and a change to the "preferences never shared" rule in `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md` and `docs/SECURITY.md`; and whether withdrawing the consent also stops the coach's reads and the keeping of the stored preferences and meal plans (GDPR Art. 17(1)(b)), beyond the flows to a model the PRD names (code review round 2's question). Its "Before a public deployment" items go to the Capstone: a DPIA covering the scope and its flow to the model provider, a processing agreement and transfer basis with OpenRouter and the providers, a privacy notice naming every recipient, a retention period for preferences and meal plans, the coach's role as (joint) controller, and AI labels if a model ever drafts or edits a meal plan. The owner asked whether long-term memory could remember things per client (2026-11-04). Sprint 3 keeps T9.7's rule (a coach's working preferences only, never about one client, checked against the clients' names), as a note a coach writes about a client is a third person's data, often health data, that the client never approved. Making the gymer the one who saves and shares answers that: the client approves every save and chooses what the coach sees. Nothing here changes the Sprint 3 tickets; the Capstone brief, not yet in the repo, decides whether these are built.

- [ ] The video says that the voice is synthetic, that the answers and the weekly review's drafts are made by an AI model and that the coach approves a draft before the gymer sees it (AC-41), and says once that the app is not medical advice; the script and the captions imply no endorsement by or affiliation with an author, journal, society, USDA, Google, OpenRouter or Streamlit, and make no claim that the app diagnoses, treats, prevents or monitors a condition (copyright rule 6) - the owner's review; the `compliance-reviewer` on the script.
- [ ] The video ends with credits naming the narration voice and its licence and giving the full URL of `ATTRIBUTION.md` on GitHub for the sources; the PR drafts the video's description with the same credits and URL, which the owner checks again at upload - the owner's review.
- [ ] The video file is not committed (it stays in T9.27's gitignored folder); the README links to it only after the owner has uploaded it - `git ls-files` lists no video file; review.
- [ ] `docs/PRD.md` ("Beyond the brief") lists the video; this plan and `docs/PROGRESS.md` record the ticket; `code-reviewer` and `compliance-reviewer` run on the PR - their reports on the PR.
**Notes:** may be done after 2026-11-23 (owner's choice), as it is for recruiters, not for the review. Uploading the video and choosing where (e.g. an unlisted YouTube video) is the owner's action. The voices ruled out on 2026-11-06: Piper's `en_US-lessac` (trained on the Blizzard 2013 data, whose terms usually allow research use only) and the Windows voices (their terms for a public video not checked). Kokoro is the first choice only if its card passes the check; compliance run 1 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#74 expects it may not, hence the fallback named above. A Capstone version can follow once the real-time chat exists.

### T9.29 In-app notifications for coaches and gymers (M, extra) · status: done · issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#76
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): core 3 (user interface: a user learns what changed without opening each page), in part; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): none; beyond the brief (owner's request, 2026-11-06).
**Depends on:** T9.12 (relationships and scopes), T9.15 (the pages), T9.16 (approved reviews), T9.23 (the sidebar)
**Acceptance criteria** (approved by the owner, 2026-11-06; after code review round 1 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#78 the same day, the owner approved three changes: the scopes granted on accepting a code and those revoked by the end of a relationship show only inside "connected" and "ended", a toast for each notification once a session, and what a gymer's deletion does to the coach's notifications; the docs criterion names the two lists of stored data; after compliance run 1 the owner chose to add its four Lows: what a read mark holds and that only the newest is kept, read marks private to the reader, a coach's deletion tested too, and the My data texts)
- [x] The notifications are worked out from rows the store already holds, with no copy of any value: a coach sees "<client> connected" (an invitation accepted, with the scopes shared at the start), "<client> started / stopped sharing <scope>" for each change after the start, and "Your relationship with <client> ended"; a gymer sees "You are connected with <coach>", "<coach> approved your weekly review for the week of <Monday>" (only an approved review about them, never a pending or rejected one) and "Your relationship with <coach> ended". The scopes granted when a code is accepted show only inside "connected", and the scopes revoked when a relationship ends only inside "ended", never as "started" or "stopped sharing" of their own (how the two are told apart, as the store's times are to the second, is settled in the Question phase). A notification shows only its kind, its time (Berlin, D21), the other side's display name (`users.display_name_of()`), the scope and the week; never a value, a metric or a review's text - unit tests on a seeded store, an accept with two scopes (one granted a second after `accepted_at`, with the clock fixed or the times seeded) and an end with two shared scopes included. Done: `src/store/notifications.py` (`list_notifications()`); a scope granted within `config.NOTIFICATIONS_SAME_EVENT_SECONDS` (5) of `accepted_at` counts as shared at the start, one revoked at `ended_at` belongs to the end (Question phase); `tests/test_store_notifications.py`: `test_the_scopes_granted_on_accept_show_only_inside_connected`, `test_the_same_event_window_is_the_config_s`, `test_a_real_accept_with_two_scopes_shows_one_connected`, `test_a_scope_turned_on_or_off_later_is_told_to_the_coach_only`, `test_an_end_with_two_shared_scopes_shows_only_ended`, `test_a_scope_stopped_before_the_end_is_its_own_event`, `test_a_gymer_sees_only_the_approved_reviews_about_them`, `test_a_former_coach_s_approved_review_keeps_their_name`, `test_a_notification_holds_no_value_or_review_text`, and after code review round 1 `test_a_scope_turned_off_and_on_within_the_window_is_told` (only a scope's first consent can be the start's) and `test_a_pair_that_connects_again_after_an_end_gets_new_events`; Berlin time in `tests/test_views_tables.py::test_a_notification_s_time_is_shown_in_berlin`.
- [x] When a gymer deletes their data (`my_data.delete_user_data()`), the coach's notifications about that gymer go with it (they are worked out from the deleted rows): the coach gets no "ended" and nothing keeps a copy (owner's choice, 2026-11-06); the same holds the other way: after a coach's deletion, the gymer's list holds nothing about that coach and raises nothing; in both cases no read mark refers to the deleted user and the other side's unread count still works - unit tests for both directions. Done: `test_a_gymer_s_deletion_takes_the_coach_s_notifications_about_them`, `test_a_coach_s_deletion_takes_the_gymer_s_notifications_about_them`.
- [x] A user sees only the notifications about a relationship they are a side of and, as a gymer, about the approved reviews about them, by the rules that already hold (both sides see the relationship and its scope history, `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md`; a gymer reads only approved reviews about them); a second coach or gymer sees none of them; a user's read marks are shown only to them and are only in their own export, so the other side never learns whether or when a notification was read; the new public store functions are classified in `tests/test_store_access.py` and use constant `?` queries (`tests/test_store_sql_scan.py`) - unit tests with a second coach, a second gymer and an ended relationship, and the coach's export after the gymer marks all as read. Done: `test_a_second_coach_or_gymer_sees_none_of_them`, `test_a_read_mark_is_the_reader_s_alone`, `test_a_missing_user_or_a_bad_id_is_refused`; `notifications.list_notifications` is own-only and `notifications.mark_all_read` a write in `tests/test_store_access.py` (not audited, owner's choice, 2026-11-06); the scan passes.
- [x] A signed-in user's sidebar shows a bell with the number of unread notifications (as text, not colour only); a Notifications page lists them newest first (at most `config.NOTIFICATIONS_MAX_SHOWN`), marks the unread ones and has "Mark all as read", which adds a read mark to a new table holding only the reader's own ID and a time (read up to), never another user's ID or the ID of another user's row, and deletes the reader's earlier marks in the same transaction, so only the newest is kept and no row is updated in place (owner's choice, 2026-11-06: an exception to keeping every row, so the table does not become a log of when the user read their notifications); each unread notification shows as a toast once a session: those unread when the session starts, and each new one on the first run that finds it (owner's choice, 2026-11-06; kept in the session, so no visit time is stored); nothing is real time: a new notification shows on the page's next run (owner's choice, 2026-11-06) - AppTest for both roles on a seeded store; a store unit test that marking all as read twice leaves one mark with the later time, and that a failed second mark keeps the first. Done: `views/notifications.py`, `notification_reads` (migration 3); `tests/test_app_notifications.py`: `test_a_coach_s_sidebar_shows_the_bell_with_the_unread_count`, `test_the_coach_s_page_lists_them_newest_first_marked_new`, `test_mark_all_as_read_clears_the_count_and_keeps_one_mark`, `test_unread_notifications_are_toasted_once_a_session_at_most_three` (at most `config.NOTIFICATIONS_MAX_TOASTS` (3) a run, the rest in one summary, owner's choice), `test_a_new_notification_is_toasted_on_the_next_run`, `test_a_gymer_sees_the_connection_and_only_the_approved_review`; store: `test_marking_twice_keeps_one_mark_with_the_later_time`, `test_a_failed_second_mark_keeps_the_first`, `test_everything_is_unread_until_marked_then_only_what_came_after`, `test_an_event_in_the_mark_s_own_second_shows_unread_once_more`; after code review round 1: `test_the_page_shows_at_most_the_configured_number`, `test_a_new_connection_on_a_reused_row_id_is_toasted` (a toast's key holds the row's time, as SQLite reuses a deleted row's ID), and the mark is read up to when the page loaded the list (`test_the_mark_covers_only_what_the_page_had_loaded`, `test_a_load_time_in_the_future_counts_as_now`).
- [x] A guest has no bell and no page; demo mode works with its synthetic users; a store error leaves the page working without the bell and shows a fixed message, never the error - AppTest. Done: `test_a_guest_has_no_bell_and_no_page`, `test_demo_users_have_their_notifications`, `test_a_store_error_leaves_the_page_working_without_the_bell`.
- [x] The new table is in `my_data.delete_user_data()` and `export_user_data()`; no log line holds a name, a scope choice or a time a user chose (`tests/test_observability.py` with canary values); nothing about a notification goes to a model - tests. Done: `tests/test_store_db.py` and `tests/test_store_my_data.py` (the new table and its user column), `test_a_read_mark_is_the_reader_s_alone` (the export); `tests/test_observability.py`'s store scenario lists and marks the canary users' notifications; `test_only_the_notification_views_read_the_notifications` (no module under `src/` imports them).
- [x] Every text is in English and German (`src/i18n.py`) - AppTest in German. Done: `test_the_bell_page_and_toasts_in_german`, `tests/test_i18n.py` (every key in both languages), `test_each_kind_reads_as_its_sentence`; after compliance run 1 (Low 1, owner's choice, 2026-11-06), the gymer's sharing texts say the coach is told of each scope change with its time and keeps seeing them after the end (`tests/test_app_sharing.py::test_the_page_says_what_the_coach_keeps_seeing_and_who_drafts_reviews`, English and German).
- [ ] `docs/PRD.md`'s feature 28 and AC-43 (written when the ticket was planned, NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#77) match what is built; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` records D23 (notifications worked out from the store's rows with a read mark, against a table that copies each event, and against email or push: no email is stored, D15) and the store section; `docs/SECURITY.md` lists the new table; the lists of stored data and of the screens where the other side's display name shows name the read marks and the notifications, in `docs/PRD.md` ("The privacy change") and `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md` ("Personal data", which also records the read marks' exception to keeping every row); the My data page's intro texts for both roles, in English and German, name the read marks (AppTest on the text); `docs/TESTING.md`, this plan and `docs/PROGRESS.md` record the ticket; `code-reviewer` and `compliance-reviewer` (personal data stored and shown, `.claude/rules/workflow.md`) run on the PR - their reports on the PR.
**Question phase (owner's choices, 2026-11-06):** both sides get "ended" (the store does not record who ended it); the list holds every event in the store, the page shows the newest `config.NOTIFICATIONS_MAX_SHOWN` (50); toasts on every page, at most `config.NOTIFICATIONS_MAX_TOASTS` (3) a run and one "and N more" toast; a coach's notification reads are not audited (own-only in `tests/test_store_access.py`). Defaults stated and kept: a scope granted within `config.NOTIFICATIONS_SAME_EVENT_SECONDS` (5) of `accepted_at` shows inside "connected", one revoked at `ended_at` inside "ended"; the read mark compares with `>=`; "ended" lists no scope. Built in its own worktree, as T9.25's uncommitted work was in the main checkout; both add migration 3 (`_V3`), so the second to merge renumbers its migration.
**Notes:** moved from the Capstone to Sprint 3 by the owner on 2026-11-06 (T9.24 had put notifications there with full messaging); the notifications of the Capstone's realtime chat stay there: a new chat message becomes one more kind in the same list, pushed over the chat's WebSocket instead of waiting for the next run (owner, 2026-11-06). No coach approval step: the invitation code is the coach's consent (D15; owner's choice, 2026-11-06). T9.25's question and answer (in T9.30, owner's choice, 2026-11-07) and T9.17's coach-set targets add their own kinds later, when those tickets are built; T9.25 itself still sends none. Left for the Question phase: whether the user who ended a relationship also gets "ended" (the store does not record who ended it); how far back the first list goes; whether the toast shows on every page; whether a coach's notification reads write an audit row (they read only rows both sides already see, like the display name, which is not audited, owner's choice, 2026-11-03), which decides their group in `tests/test_store_access.py`; how a read mark tells two events in the same second apart (the store's times are to the second, so a strict "read up to" can miss or repeat one): the mark holds only a time, so the answer is time-based (a row or sequence ID is ruled out by the rule on what a mark holds), e.g. a `>=` comparison under which an event in the mark's own second shows as unread once more; whether the toasts are capped (e.g. at `config.NOTIFICATIONS_MAX_SHOWN`, the rest as one summary toast) when many notifications are unread at the start of a session. The compliance review (run 1 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#78) also asks that the build put no user, relationship or review ID in a URL or query parameter, cache no notification in `st.cache_data` or `st.cache_resource` (shared across sessions), and keep the toast state in `st.session_state` only; and that each later kind (T9.25, T9.17) goes only to a side who may read what it is about and holds no text or value.

### T9.30 Notifications of a new question or answer (S, extra) · status: done · issue NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#87
**Serves:** Sprint 3 (`docs/BRIEF_SPRINT3.md`): core 3 (user interface: a user learns what changed without opening each page), in part; Sprint 2 (`docs/BRIEF.md`): none; beyond the brief (owner's request, 2026-11-06, recorded in T9.25's Notes; this ticket owns it by the owner's choice, 2026-11-07, code review round 4 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#80).
**Depends on:** T9.25 (the questions and answers), T9.29 (the notifications list, the bell, the read marks and the toasts)
**Acceptance criteria** (proposed by Claude, 2026-11-07; approved by the owner, 2026-11-08, with the middle way for the audit in the second criterion; the owner confirmed the same day the wording code review rounds 1 and 2 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#89 led to, the third criterion's allowed changes to T9.29's page included)
- [x] Two new kinds in T9.29's list, worked out from the `review_questions` rows with no copy of any value: a coach sees "<client> asked a question under the weekly review for the week of <Monday>", and a gymer sees "<coach> answered your question under the weekly review for the week of <Monday>"; each shows only its kind, its time (Berlin, D21), the other side's display name (`users.display_name_of()`) and the week, never the question's or the answer's text - unit tests on a seeded store with a canary question and answer. Done: `questions.list_question_events()` (IDs, the week and the time, never the text) and the kinds `question_asked` and `question_answered` in `notifications.list_notifications()`; `tests/test_store_question_notifications.py`: `test_a_coach_is_told_of_a_question_and_a_gymer_of_an_answer`, `test_a_question_waiting_for_its_answer_is_told_to_the_coach_only`, `test_each_question_and_answer_is_its_own_event`, `test_a_question_notification_holds_no_text` (canary texts); Berlin time and the sentences in `tests/test_views_tables.py::test_each_kind_reads_as_its_sentence`.
- [x] Each goes only to a side who may read the thread by the rule of the review it belongs to (T9.25): the coach while the relationship is active and the client shares nutrition and every scope the review used (an ended relationship or a scope turned off hides the coach's question notifications, as it hides the thread); the gymer for the answers under the approved reviews about them, a former coach's included, as they still read those threads (owner's choice, 2026-11-06); a second coach or gymer sees none - unit tests with a second coach, a second gymer, an ended relationship and a scope the review used turned off; the coach's "asked a question" notifications are read by a store function that checks `access.authorise_read()` (nutrition) and reads `open_shares` for every scope the review used, so the read runs through `tests/test_store_access.py`'s refused-cases matrix (the same `NotFound` for every refused case); it is audited (a `read` of `review_questions`, `access.audit()`, no values) only when the coach opens the Notifications page, never for the sidebar's count or a toast, which run on every page (the middle way, owner's choice, 2026-11-08, so the audit is not filled with page loads); this exception to `authorised_read()` auditing every read is recorded in `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md` and `docs/SECURITY.md` - unit tests: the refused-cases matrix covers the function the bell, the toasts and the page call, for a coach reading a client (as it covers `list_questions`); the coach's own list, which is filtered, never refused, is tested as `list_questions`' own list is in `tests/test_store_questions.py` (an ended relationship, a scope turned off, another coach's client); the Notifications page writes one `read` audit row of `review_questions` per client whose question notification it lists, its `subject_id` that client (so the client sees it in their export, as for `list_questions`), and the count and the toasts write none (clarified after code review round 1 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#89, within the owner's approval). Done: `questions.list_question_events` in `READS` of `tests/test_store_access.py` (every refused case, a scope not shared, a revoke, an end, one audit row by default), and `test_the_unaudited_question_events_read_is_refused_the_same_way`; the coach's own list: `test_an_ended_relationship_hides_the_coach_s_but_not_the_gymer_s`, `test_a_scope_the_review_used_turned_off_hides_the_coach_s` (nutrition and a scope the review used), `test_a_second_coach_or_gymer_sees_none_of_them`, `test_another_coach_s_client_is_not_found`; the audit: `test_the_count_and_the_toasts_write_no_audit_row`, `test_the_page_writes_one_read_row_per_client_listed`, `test_a_coach_with_no_question_or_a_gymer_writes_no_audit_row`, `test_the_client_sees_the_page_s_audit_row_in_their_export`, `test_a_coach_reading_one_client_is_always_audited` (after code review round 1, `audited` skips only the coach's own list's rows); "per client whose question notification it lists" is met as "per client whose question the page's store read found": the page shows the newest 50, so a client further down still gets a row, and a page that fails after the read keeps its rows (over-reporting, never under-reporting; a known limit in `docs/SECURITY.md`, code review round 1); in the UI `tests/test_app_question_notifications.py::test_only_the_page_writes_the_coach_s_read_audit_row` (none for the bell and the toasts, one row for Alex per run of the page) and `test_a_gymer_s_page_writes_no_audit_row`; the exception recorded in `.claude/rules/security-privacy.md` ("The store") and `docs/SECURITY.md` (the questions row, the authorisation row, a known limit).
- [x] They use T9.29's read marks, unread count, toasts and page (no new table, no new stored data; T9.29's list gains the two kinds, and the page's one new step is telling the store that its read is audited, settled in the Question phase); when either side deletes their data, the notifications about their questions and answers go with the rows - unit tests; AppTest that a new question raises the coach's count and an answer the gymer's. Done: no migration and no new table; `views/notifications.py`'s page passes `audited=True`, the bell and the toasts nothing; `test_the_question_kinds_use_the_read_mark` (both sides), `test_a_gymer_s_deletion_takes_the_coach_s_question_notifications`, `test_a_coach_s_deletion_takes_the_gymer_s_answer_notifications`; AppTest `test_a_new_question_raises_the_coach_s_count_and_toasts`, `test_an_answer_raises_the_gymer_s_count_and_toasts`.

- [x] Every text is in English and German (`src/i18n.py`) - AppTest in German. Done: `test_the_question_and_answer_lines_in_german`, `test_the_coach_s_question_line_and_intro_in_german`, `tests/test_i18n.py` (every key in both languages).
- [ ] `docs/PRD.md` (feature 28 and AC-43 list the two kinds; "The privacy change" no longer says that reading notifications is never audited), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (D23 and the notifications paragraph), `src/store/notifications.py`'s docstring and the `OWN_ONLY` comment in `tests/test_store_access.py` (which say notification reads are not audited), and the My data and sharing texts that list what the notifications show match what is built; this plan and `docs/PROGRESS.md` record the ticket; `code-reviewer` and `compliance-reviewer` (personal data shown in a new place, `.claude/rules/workflow.md`) run on the PR - their reports on the PR. Updated: PRD (features 27 and 28, AC-43, "The privacy change"), ARCHITECTURE (D23 and the notifications paragraph), `src/store/notifications.py`'s and `src/store/questions.py`'s docstrings, the `OWN_ONLY` comment, the Notifications page's intros and the sharing page's text (English and German; `test_the_sharing_page_says_the_coach_is_told_of_a_question`; the My data intros list stored data, not what notifications show, so they stay), TESTING, this plan and PROGRESS; the reviews' reports are on the PR.
**Question phase (owner's choices, 2026-11-09):** the page's audit is written on each run of the page, Streamlit's reruns included (no session state); the page tells the store through an argument, `list_notifications(..., audited=True)`, passed to `questions.list_question_events()`, which writes the audit in the same transaction as its read (that read runs before `list_notifications()`' own transaction, as a store read cannot open one inside another); an unanswered question shows once, at the time it was asked, like every T9.29 notification (Client week's line names the weeks that wait); each question or answer row is its own event, keyed by its kind, ID and time, with no same-event window (the read mark's `>=` as in T9.29; within one second the order is approved, asked, answered, ended). Built in its own worktree (`.claude/worktrees/t9.30`), as another session's T8.6 work was in the main checkout.
**Notes:** split out of T9.25 so its PR keeps its scope (owner's choice, 2026-11-07). **The owner's approval (2026-11-08):** the criteria as proposed, with the middle way for the audit (the second criterion), chosen from (a) a `read` audit row on every read, (b) own-only and not audited like T9.29's other kinds, and the middle way (T9.29 audits no notification read, as they hold only what both sides already see; a question notification reads a client's thread). The cost of (a) that led to it: the sidebar reads the notifications on every run of every page, so (a) writes a coach `read` audit row per client with a thread on each run (e.g. 5 clients and 200 clicks make 1,000 rows, kept until deletion and in both exports), and the audit no longer tells a real read of a thread from a page load. The middle way, as it was put to the owner (code review round 6 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#80): the read runs through the refused-cases matrix, but it is audited only when the Notifications page opens. Left for the Question phase: whether the page's audit is written on each run of the page (Streamlit reruns it on every click) or once a session; how the page tells the store its read is audited (an argument to `list_notifications`, or its own call), as a store read cannot open a transaction inside `list_notifications`' (code review round 1 of NMH1988/fitcoach-ai-assistant#89); whether a question still unanswered shows once or until it is answered; and how two rows in the same second are told apart (T9.29's `config.NOTIFICATIONS_SAME_EVENT_SECONDS` rule).
